He further thanks A. Engel for motivating discussion and suggesting that
the uncompleted topological
K
-homology might be an interesting object to consider.

2
Branched coarse coverings

In this section we introduce the concept of a branched coarse covering.
We work in the category
\mathbf{BC}
of bornological coarse spaces introduced in
[
BE20b
]
.

In order to state the basic definition we first introduce some notation. The complement
of a subset
Z
of a set
Y
will be denoted by
Z^{c}:=Y\setminus Z
.
The right-restriction of an entourage
V
on
Y
to a subset
A
will be denoted by
V_{A}:=V\cap(Y\times A)
. The preimage of the entourage under a map
f:X\to Y
will be written as
f^{-1}(V):=(f\times f)^{-1}(V)
.

We now consider two bornological coarse spaces
X
and
Y
and a map

f:X\to Y
between the underlying sets. Furthermore, we consider a big family

{\mathcal{Z}}
on
Y
.

Definition 2.1
.

The pair
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
is called a branched coarse covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
if it has the following properties:

1.

f
is controlled.

2.

f
is bornological and has locally finite fibres.

3.

There exists an entourage
P
of
X
(called a coarse connection) such that:

(a)

For every coarse entourage
V
of
Y
there exists member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}

such that:

i.

for every

(y^{\prime},y)
in
V_{Z^{c}}
and
x
in
f^{-1}(y)
there exists a unique

x^{\prime}
in
f^{-1}(y^{\prime})
such that
(x^{\prime},x)\in P
.

ii.

The subset

P\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})
is a coarse entourage of
X
.

(b)

For every coarse entourage
U
of
X
there exists a member
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that

U_{f^{-1}(Z^{c})}\subseteq P
.

Note that
P
is not necessarily a coarse entourage of
X
,
and that
f
is in general not a morphism in
G\mathbf{BC}

since it is not proper.

Remark 2.2
.

In the situation of

Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
we say that the parallel transport at scale
V
is defined on
Z^{c}
.
This notion is motivated as follows. A discrete
V
-controlled path
in
Z^{c}
is a finite sequence
\gamma:=(y_{0},\dots,y_{n})
of points in
Z^{c}
such that

(y_{i},y_{i+1})\in V
for all
i
in
\{0,\dots,n-1\}
.
For every

(y^{\prime},y)
in
V_{Z^{c}}
we have a canonical bijection
\Phi_{y^{\prime},y}:f^{-1}(y)\to f^{-1}(y^{\prime})
determined by the condition that
P\cap(f^{-1}(y^{\prime})\times f^{-1}(y))

is the graph of
\Phi_{y^{\prime},y}
.
The composition

\Phi_{\gamma}:\Phi_{y_{n},y_{n-1}}\circ\dots\circ\Phi_{y_{1},y_{0}}:f^{-1}(y_{0})\to f^{-1}(y_{n})

(2.1)

will be considered as a parallel transport along the path
\gamma
.
∎

The following lemma shows that the connection of a branched coarse covering is essentially unique.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a branched coarse covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Lemma 2.3
.

If
P,P^{\prime}
are two connections for
f
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
, then for every coarse entourage
V
of
Y
there exists a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}

such that

P\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})=P^{\prime}\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for every coarse entourage
V
of
Y
there exists a sufficiently large member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
P\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z})\subseteq P^{\prime}
.

We first observe that the conditions in
Definition

2.1
.
3a
and

Definition

2.1
.
3b
are preserved under enlarging the member
Z
.
Let
V
be a coarse entourage of
Y
and let
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
be such that
Definition

2.1
.
3
is satisfied for
P
.
Then by
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)ii
the set
P\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z})
is a coarse entourage of
X
.
After possibly enlarging
Z
, by
Definition

2.1
.
3b
for
P^{\prime}
we have
P\cap f^{-1}(V_{Z})\subseteq P^{\prime}
.
∎

It follows from
Lemma

2.3
that for every every coarse entourage
V
of
Y
there exists a member
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that the parallel transports for

V
-controlled paths in
Z^{c}
defined by
P
and
P^{\prime}
coincide.

For a coarse space we let
\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(X)
denote the set of coarse components of
X
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of bornological coarse spaces
and recall the notions of a coarse covering from
[
BCKW
, Def. 3.3.16]
and of a bounded coarse covering from
[
BEKW20c
]
,
[
BCKW
, Def. 3.4.16]
.

Lemma 2.4
.

If
f
is a coarse branched covering relative to the big family
(\emptyset)
, then
it is a coarse covering.
In particular, the underlying map of coarse spaces restricted to any coarse component
Y_{i}
of
Y

is isomorphic to the projection
I_{min}\otimes Y_{i}\to Y_{i}
for some set
I
.

If in addition for every bounded set
B
of
X
the map
\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(B)\to\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(f(B))
has uniformly finite fibres, then
f
is a bounded coarse covering

Proof.

Let
f_{*}:\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)\to\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)
denote the induced map between the sets of coarse components.
We first verify the condition
[
BCKW
, Def. 3.4.16(1)]
requiring that the restriction of
f
to any coarse component
X_{i}
of
X
is an isomorphism from
X_{i}
to the coarse component
Y_{f_{*}i}
.
Let
P
be a connection for
f
.
It is clear that
f(X_{i})
is contained in
Y_{f_{*}i}
. Let
x
be in
X_{i}
and set
y:=f(x)
in
Y_{f_{*}i}
. If
y^{\prime}
is any other point in
Y_{i}
, then there exists an entourage
V
of
Y
such that

(y^{\prime},y)\in V
. Then
f^{-1}(V)\cap P
is by
Definition

2.1
.
3a
a coarse entourage of
X
and we have the parallel transport

x^{\prime}
in
f^{-1}(y^{\prime})
of
x
at scale
V
. Since
X_{i}
is coarsely connected we have
x^{\prime}\in X_{i}
and hence
y^{\prime}\in f(X_{i})
. If
x^{\prime\prime}
is any point in
f^{-1}(y^{\prime})\cap X_{i}
, then there exists an entourage
U
of
X
such that
(x^{\prime\prime},x)\in U
.
Then
f(U)
is a coarse entourage of
Y
and
x^{\prime\prime}
is the parallel transport of
x
at scale
f(U)
. Both,
x^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime\prime}
are then parallel transports of
x
at scale
f(U)\cup V
and hence equal.
We conclude that
the restriction
f_{|X_{i}}:X_{i}\to Y_{f_{*}i}
bijective.

For every coarse entourage
V
of
Y
we have
V=f(f^{-1}(V)\cap P)
, and for every coarse entourage
U
of
X

we have
U=f^{-1}(f(U))\cap P
by
Definition

2.1
.
3b
. This implies that
f_{|X_{i}}:X_{i}\to Y_{f_{*}i}
is an isomorphism of coarse spaces. Furthermore,
P=U(\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)):=\bigcup_{U\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}}U
(here
U(\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X))
is the notation from
[
BCKW
]
) and the coarse structure of
X
is generated by
the entourages of the form
f^{-1}(V)\cap P
for all coarse entourages
V
of
Y
.

This finishes the proof of the first assertion, see also
[
BCKW
, Ex. 3.3.18]
for the second assertion.

For the last assertion, the additional condition on the bornology
is precisely the restriction on the bornologies of the domains of bounded coarse coverings.
∎

Example 2.5
.

Let
I
be a set and
Y
be a bornological coarse space. Then the projection

f:I_{min,min}\otimes Y\to Y
is a bounded coarse covering and therefore a branched coarse covering
relative to the big family
(\emptyset)
.
∎

Example 2.6
.

We consider a proper path metric space
Y
and let

X\to Y
be a topological covering. We equip
X
with the induced path metric
such the projection map is a local isometry. Then
X
is again a proper metric space.

We consider
X

and
Y
with the bornological coarse structures induced by the metrics.
For
r
in
[0,\infty)
we let
V_{r}
denote the metric entourage of
Y
of width
r
.
Similarly the metric entourages of
X
will be denoted by
U_{r}
.

For
r
in
(0,\infty)
a metric space is called
r
-separated of any two distinct points have distance
>r
.
We let
Z_{r}
be the subset of points
y
of
Y
such that the fibre
f^{-1}(y)
is not
r
-separated.

Lemma 2.7
.

The family
{\mathcal{Z}}:=(Z_{r})_{r\in[0,\infty)}
is a big family.

Proof.

We have
V_{s}[Z_{r}]\subseteq Z_{r+2s}
. ∎

We call
{\mathcal{Z}}
the canonical big family associated to the covering.

Lemma 2.8
.

The map
f:X\to Y
is a coarse branched covering relative to the canonical big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

The map
f
is
1
-Lipschitz and therefore controlled and bornological.

The fibres of
f
are locally finite since they are discrete and
X
is proper.

We choose the connection

P:=\bigcup_{r\in[0,\infty)}(U_{r})_{f^{-1}(Z^{c}_{2r})}\ .

The condition from
Definition

2.1
.
3
. is satisfied since for the metric entourage

V_{s}
of
Y
we can take the member

Z_{2s}
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
∎

Example 2.9
.

Let
(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a sequence of simplicial complexes. We consider
Y_{n}
as a path-metric space
with the spherical path metric. Let
X_{n}\to Y_{n}
be the universal covering with the induced path metric.
We form the bounded unions
Y:=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}^{\mathrm{bd}}Y_{n}
and

X:=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}^{\mathrm{bd}}X_{n}
in
\mathbf{BC}
and let
f:X\to Y
be the canonical map.
We furthermore consider the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}:=(Y_{\leq n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with
Y_{\leq n}:=\bigcup_{m\leq n}Y_{m}
.
Let
\ell(n)
denote the minimum of the length of non-contractible loops in
Y_{n}
.

Lemma 2.10
.

If
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\to\infty}\ell(n)=\infty
, then
f:X\to Y
is a branched coarse covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
∎

Example 2.11
.

The asymptotically faithful covering sequences of spaces of graphs in
[
WY12
, Def. 2.2]

give rise to branched coarse coverings.
∎

In the following we introduce the equivariant generalization of the concept of a branched coarse covering.
We let
G
be a group and refer to
[
BEKW20a
]
for the category
G\mathbf{BC}
of
G
-bornological coarse spaces.
We consider two
G
-bornological coarse spaces
X
and
Y
and an equivariant map
f:X\to Y
between the the underlying sets and an
invariant big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Definition 2.12
.

1.

f
is a
G
-equivariant coarse branched covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
if it is
a coarse branched covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
for a
G
-invariant connection.

2.

If there exists
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
G
acts freely and transitively on the fibres of
f_{|f^{-1}(Z^{c})}
, then
we call
f
a branched coarse
G
-covering.

This condition implies that the parallel transport is also compatible with the
G
-action.

Example 2.13
.

Let
Y
be a bornological coarse space and

I
be a
G
-set. We consider
I_{min,min}\otimes Y
as a
G
-bornological coarse space, where
Y
has the trivial
G
-action.
Then the projection
I_{min,min}\otimes Y\to Y
is a
G
-equivariant branched coarse covering
relative to the big family
(\emptyset)
.
If
I=G
, then it is a branched coarse
G
-covering.

∎

Example 2.14
.

We consider the subspace
Y:=\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid\|y\|\geq 1\}
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and let
X\to Y

be the universal covering with its canonical
{\mathbb{Z}}
-action. We equip
Y
with the path metric structure induced by the euclidean metric on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
Since
Y
is a proper path-metric space, by
Example

2.6

the map
f:X\to Y
is a branched coarse
{\mathbb{Z}}
-covering relative to the big family
of
{\mathcal{Z}}:=(B(0,r)\cap Y)_{r\geq 0}
.
∎

Example 2.15
.

Let
G
be a group and
(G_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
be a decreasing family of normal cofinite subgroups such that

\bigcap_{n\in{\rm Nature}}G_{n}=\{e\}
. We form the
G
-set

S:=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}G/G_{n}

and
the
G
-bornological coarse space

X:=G_{can,min}\otimes S_{min,min}\ .

We let
X_{n}
denote the subset
G\times G/G_{n}
of
X
.

We consider the set

Y:=G\backslash X

with the minimal coarse structure
such that
f:X\to Y
is controlled, and the minimal compatible bornology such that the projection
f:X\to Y
is bornological.
We let
Y_{n}:=f(X_{n})\cong G/G_{n}
and define the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}:=(Y_{\leq n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
with
Y_{\leq n}:=\bigcup_{m\leq n}Y_{n}
.

Lemma 2.16
.

If
G
is countably generated, then the map
f:X\to Y
is a branched coarse
G
-covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

The map
f
is controlled and has locally finite fibres.
The group
G
acts freely and transitively on the fibres of
f
.

Since
G
is countably generated there exists a cofinal sequence
(U_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of symmetric invariant coarse entourages of
G_{can}
such that
G_{n}
is
U^{2}_{n}
-discrete for every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
. Then we define the coarse connection by

P:=\bigcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}U_{n}\times{\mathtt{diag}}(G/G_{n})\ .

The condition in
Definition

2.1
.
3b
follows from the cofinality of
(U_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
.
Note that the
coarse entourages
V=f(U)
for invariant coarse entourages
U
of
X

are cofinal in the coarse structure of
Y
. To this end we observe that
f(U)\circ f(U^{\prime})=f(U\circ U^{\prime})
for invariant entourages
U,U^{\prime}
.

We fix an invariant coarse entourage
U
of
X
and consider the coarse entourage
V:=f(U)
of
Y
.
We take
m
such that
U\cap(X_{m}\times X_{m})\subseteq U_{m}
and check that
the condition from
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
holds true for the member
Y_{\leq m}
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
. Let
n
be in
{\rm Nature}
with
n>m
.
Let
(g,g^{\prime}G_{n})
be in
X_{n}
and assume that
(\tilde{g},\tilde{g}^{\prime}G_{n})
is such that

(g,\tilde{g})\in U_{n}
and
g^{\prime}G_{n}=\tilde{g}^{\prime}G_{n}
so that

([g,g^{\prime}G_{n}],[\tilde{g},\tilde{g}^{\prime}G_{n}])\in f(U)\cap(Y_{n}\times Y_{n})
. We can assume that
g^{\prime}=\tilde{g}^{\prime}
.
Let
h
be in
G
such that
((g,g^{\prime}G_{n}),(h\tilde{g},hg^{\prime}G_{n}))\in P\cap(X_{n}\times X_{n})
. We must show that this implies
h=e
.
First of all
hg^{\prime}G_{n}=g^{\prime}G_{n}
implies that
h\in G_{n}
by normality
of
G_{n}
. Furthermore, by the symmetry and
G
-invariance of
U_{n}
the relations
(g,\tilde{g})\in U_{n}
and
(g,h\tilde{g})\in U_{n}
imply
(e,\tilde{g}^{-1}h\tilde{g})\in U^{2}_{n}
. Since
\tilde{g}^{-1}h\tilde{g}\in G_{n}

and
G_{n}
is
U_{n}^{2}
-discrete we can conclude that
\tilde{g}^{-1}h\tilde{g}=e
, hence
h=e
.

In order to check the condition in
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)ii
we observe that
U_{f^{-1}(Z_{n}^{c})}\cap P=U_{f^{-1}(Z_{n}^{c})}
.
∎

In the following we discuss the base-change of branched coarse coverings.
For a bornological coarse space
X
we let
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{X}
denote the coarse structure and the bornology of
X
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a branched coarse covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Let
h:Y^{\prime}\to Y
be a morphism of bornological coarse spaces.
We form the pull-back

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y}

(2.2)

in coarse spaces. We furthermore equip
X^{\prime}
with the bornology
generated by
g^{-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{X})
.
We consider the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}:=h^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}})
on
Y^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.17
.

f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is a branched coarse covering relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

First of all
f^{\prime}
is controlled by definition. Furthermore, the bornology on
X^{\prime}
is compatible with the coarse structure.

We next show that
f^{\prime}
has locally finite fibres.
Let
y^{\prime}
be in
Y^{\prime}
and assume that
B
in
X
is bounded. Then

g^{-1}(B)\cap f^{\prime,-1}(y^{\prime})\subseteq\{y^{\prime}\}\times(f^{-1}(h(y))\cap B)

is indeed finite.

Since
f^{\prime}(g^{-1}(B))\subseteq h^{-1}(f(B))
we also see that
f^{\prime}
is bornological.

Let
P
be the connection for
f
. Then we set
P^{\prime}:=g^{-1}(P)
.

We now check that
P^{\prime}
is a connection for
f^{\prime}
.
Let
V^{\prime}
be a coarse entourage of
Y^{\prime}
.
Then
V=h(V)
is a coarse entourage of
Y
and we can choose the member

Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
for
V
such that the
Definition

2.1
.
3

is satisfied with
P
. We then claim that this condition is satisfied for

V^{\prime}
and
Z^{\prime}:=f^{-1}(Z)
with
P^{\prime}
.

We start with
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
.
Let
(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime})
be in
V^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime,c}}
and
x^{\prime}_{0}
be in
f^{\prime,-1}(y_{0}^{\prime})
.
We set
y_{i}:=h(y_{i}^{\prime})
for
i=0,1
and
x_{0}:=g(x_{0}^{\prime})
. Then there exists a unique

x_{1}
in
f^{-1}(y_{1})
such that
(x_{1},x_{0})\in P
.
Since the underlying square of sets in (
2.2
) is cartesian
there exists a unique

x_{1}^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
such that
f^{\prime}(x_{1}^{\prime})=y_{1}^{\prime}
and
g(x_{1}^{\prime})=x_{1}
.
By definition of
P^{\prime}
we have
(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{0}^{\prime})\in P^{\prime}
.
For uniqeness, assume that

x_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is in
f^{\prime,-1}(y_{1}^{\prime})
and
(x_{1}^{\prime\prime},x_{0}^{\prime})\in P^{\prime}
. Then

(g(x_{1}^{\prime\prime}),g(x_{0}^{\prime}))\in P
,
g(x_{1}^{\prime\prime})\in f^{-1}(y_{1})
and hence

g(x_{1}^{\prime\prime})=g(x_{1}^{\prime})
. Since also
f^{\prime}(x_{1}^{\prime\prime})=f^{\prime}(x_{1}^{\prime})
we conclude that
x_{1}^{\prime}=x_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.

We now verify
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)ii
.
Note that
f^{\prime}(f^{\prime,-1}(V^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime,c}})\cap P^{\prime})\subseteq V^{\prime}
and

g(f^{\prime,-1}(V^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime,c}})\cap P^{\prime})\subseteq f^{-1}(V_{Z})\cap P
.
Since
V^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{Y^{\prime}}
and
f^{-1}(V_{Z})\cap P\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}

by the choice of
Z
we conclude that
that
f^{\prime,-1}(V^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime,c}})\cap P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X^{\prime}}
since the square in
(
2.2
) is cartesian in coarse spaces.

We finally verify
Definition

2.1
.
3b
.
Let
U^{\prime}
be in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X^{\prime}}
. Then there exists a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that

g(U^{\prime})_{f^{-1}(Z)}\in P
. Applying
g^{-1}
we conclude that

U^{\prime}_{f^{\prime,-1}(Z^{\prime})}\subseteq P^{\prime}
if we set
Z^{\prime}:=h^{-1}(Z)
.
∎

Remark 2.18
.

For a pair
(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
of a bornological coarse space and a big family
the coarse corona
\partial_{{\mathcal{Y}}}X
was introduced in

[
BL24
]
as the Gelfand dual of the quotient of the algebra of bounded functions on
X
whose variation
vanishes away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
by the ideal generated by functions supported on
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

We now consider a coarse branched covering
f:X\to Y
relative to the family
{\mathcal{Z}}
. We then get an induced map

\partial f:\partial_{f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}})}X\to\partial_{{\mathcal{Z}}}Y

of coronas.
∎

3
Uniform coverings, cones and Rips complexes

We work in the context of uniform bornological coarse spaces
\mathbf{UBC}
introduced in

[
BE20b
]
.

For a uniform space
X
we let
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
denote the poset of uniform entourages of
X
with the opposite of the inclusion relation.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a map between the underlying sets of
uniform bornological coarse spaces.

Definition 3.1
.

The map
f:X\to Y
is called a uniform covering if:

1.

f
is controlled and uniform.

2.

f
is bornological and has locally finite fibres.

3.

There exists an entourage
P
of
X
(called the connection) such that:

(a)

For every
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
the entourage
f^{-1}(U)\cap P
is uniform and coarse.

(b)

There exists
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
such that for every
y
in
Y

the map
f
restricts to a
uniform homeomorphism

\tilde{U}[f^{-1}(y)]\cong f^{-1}(U[y])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}f^{-1}(y)_{disc}\otimes U[y]\ ,

where
\tilde{U}:=f^{-1}(U)\cap P
.

(c)

The set
\{f^{-1}(U)\cap P\mid U\in{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}\}
is cofinal in
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

If
P
and
P^{\prime}
are two connections for
f
, then there exist uniform entourages
U
and
U^{\prime}
of
Y
such that

f^{-1}(U)\cap P\subseteq P^{\prime}
and
f^{-1}(U^{\prime})\cap P^{\prime}\subseteq P
.

Proof.

Let
U_{0}
be in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
. Then
f^{-1}(U_{0})\cap P^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
by
Definition

3.1
.
3a
. Then by
Definition

3.1
.
3c
there exists
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
such that

f^{-1}(U)\cap P\subseteq f^{-1}(U_{0})\cap P^{\prime}\subseteq P^{\prime}
.
∎

Assume that
X
and
Y
are
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces and that
f
is equivariant.

Definition 3.3
.

1.

f
is a
G
-equivariant uniform covering if
f
is a uniform covering with a
G
-invariant connection
P
.

2.

f
is a uniform
G
-covering if it is a
G
-equivariant uniform covering
and
G
acts freely and transitively on the fibres of
f
.

Example 3.4
.

In the situation of
Example

2.6
assume that
there exists an
r
in
(0,\infty)
such that all fibres of
f
are
r
-discrete.
This happens e.g. if
Y
is compact.
Then

f:X\to Y
is a uniform covering. For the connection
P
we can take the metric entourage
U_{r/2}
of
X
.
∎

Example 3.5
.

The covering
X\to Y=\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid\|y\|\geq 1\}
from
Example

2.14
is a uniform
{\mathbb{Z}}
-covering.
The analogous construction with
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}:=\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid\|y\|>0\}
does not yield
a uniform covering since condition
Definition

3.1
.
3b
is violated.

∎

Let
X
be a
G
-uniform bornological coarse space. We first introduce the construction of the squeezing space
\hat{X}_{h}
.
We start with the bounded union

\hat{X}:=\bigsqcup_{{\rm Nature}}^{\mathrm{bd}}X

(3.1)

in
G\mathbf{UBC}
.
We let
X_{n}
denote the
n
-th component of
\hat{X}
.
The uniform and the coarse structure of
\hat{X}
are generated by entourages of the form

\bigsqcup_{{\rm Nature}}U
for uniform or coarse entourages
U
of
X
, respectively. The bornology of
\hat{X}
is generated by bounded subsets of
X
placed in one of the components.
On
\hat{X}
we consider the invariant big family
\hat{\mathcal{Y}}:=(\hat{X}_{\leq n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
given by
\hat{X}_{\leq n}:=\bigsqcup_{m\leq n}X_{m}
.

Recall the construction of hybrid coarse structures from
[
BE20b
, Sec. 5.1]
.

Definition 3.6
.

The squeezing space
\hat{X}_{h}
is the
G
-bornological coarse space obtained from
\hat{X}
by equipping it with the
hybrid coarse structure with respect to the big family
\hat{\mathcal{Y}}
.

By definition a coarse entourage of
\hat{X}
belongs to the hybrid structure if for every uniform entourage
V
of
X
there exists
m
in
{\rm Nature}
such that we have

U\cap(X_{n}\times X_{n})\subseteq V
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
with
n\geq m
.
The identity of underlying sets induces a morphism
\hat{X}_{h}\to\iota\hat{X}
,
where
\iota:G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{BC}
is the forgetful functor.

The construction of the squeezing space extends to a
functor

X\mapsto\hat{X}_{h}:G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{BC}

in the obvious way. We furthermore have a natural transformation

(\hat{-})_{h}\to(\hat{-}):G\mathbf{UBC}\to G\mathbf{BC}\ .

Let
f:X\to Y
be a controlled, bornological and uniform (but not necessarily proper) map between
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces.
We then get a controlled and bornological map

\hat{f}:\hat{X}_{h}\to\hat{Y}_{h}

between the associated squeezing spaces.
We consider the invariant big family

\hat{\mathcal{Z}}:=(\hat{Y}_{\leq n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\ ,\quad\hat{Y}_{\leq n}:=\bigcup_{m\leq n}Y_{m}

(3.2)

on
\hat{Y}_{h}

Lemma 3.7
.

If
f
is a uniform covering, then the map
\hat{f}:\hat{X}_{h}\to\hat{Y}_{h}
is a
branched coarse covering with respect to the big family
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

Definition

2.1
.
2
is clearly satisfied.
The map
\hat{f}:\hat{X}\to\hat{Y}
is controlled and uniform. Since

\hat{X}_{h}
is defined using the induced big family
f^{-1}(\hat{\mathcal{Z}})
the map

\hat{X}_{h}\to\hat{Y}_{h}
is also controlled
by the functoriality of the construction of hybrid structures. Hence
Definition

2.1
.
1

is satisfied.

Using the connection
P
for
f
we define the coarse connection for

\hat{f}
by
Q:=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}P
.

Let
V
be a coarse entourage of
\hat{Y}_{h}
.
Let
U
be a uniform entourage of
Y
as in
Definition

3.1
.
3b
.
Then we choose
m
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
V\cap(Y_{n}\times Y_{n})\subseteq U
for all
n\geq m+1
.
Then
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
is satisfied for the member
\hat{Y}_{\leq m}
of
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
.

By
Definition

3.1
.
3a
it is clear that
\hat{f}^{-1}(V_{\hat{Y}_{\leq m}^{c}})\cap Q

is a coarse entourage of
\hat{X}
.
We must show that it also belongs to the hybrid structure.
Let
\tilde{U}
be a uniform entourage of
X
. Then by
Definition

3.1
.
3c
there exists a uniform entourage

U^{\prime}\subseteq U
of
Y
such that
f^{-1}(U^{\prime})\cap P\subseteq\tilde{U}
.
Since
V
is a hybrid entourage there exists
m^{\prime}\geq m
such that
V\cap(Y_{n}\times Y_{n})\subseteq U^{\prime}
for all
n\geq m^{\prime}+1
.
Then

\hat{f}^{-1}(V_{\hat{Z}^{c}_{\leq m}})\cap Q\cap(X_{n}\times X_{n})\subseteq\tilde{U}
for all
n\geq m^{\prime}
.
This shows that
\hat{f}^{-1}(V_{\hat{Z}_{\leq m}^{c}})\cap Q
belongs to the hybrid structure. We thus have verified

Finally let
\hat{V}
be a coarse entourage of
\hat{X}_{h}
.
We choose a uniform entourage
U
of
Y
.
Then there exists
k
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
\hat{V}\cap(X_{n}\times X_{n})\subseteq f^{-1}(U)\cap P
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
with
n\geq k+1
.
This implies that
Definition

2.1
.
3b
.

\hat{V}_{X_{\leq k}^{c}}\subseteq Q
. We have verified

Definition

2.1
.
3b
.
∎

Corollary 3.8
.

1.

If
f
is a
G
-equivariant uniform covering, then
\hat{f}:\hat{X}_{h}\to\hat{Y}_{h}
is a
G
-equivariant branced coarse
G
-covering with respect to
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
.

2.

If
f:X\to Y
is a uniform
G
-covering, then
\hat{f}:\hat{X}_{h}\to\hat{Y}_{h}
is a branched coarse
G
-covering with respect to
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Construction 3.9
.

We recall the cone construction
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{BC}
.
For
Y
in
\mathbf{UBC}
the underlying bornological space of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
is
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes Y
on which
we consider the big family
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y):=({\mathcal{O}}_{\leq n}(Y))_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
given by the subsets
{\mathcal{O}}_{\leq n}(Y):=(-\infty,n)\times Y
.
The coarse structure of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
is the hybrid associated to
the coarse and uniform structure on
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes Y
and the big family
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
. Thus a coarse entourage
V
of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes Y
is hybrid, if for every uniform entourage
U
of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes Y
there exists

n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that

V_{|{\mathcal{O}}(Y)_{\leq n}^{c}}\subseteq U
.
∎

We define a map of sets
\hat{i}:\hat{X}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})

which restricts to
X_{n}\ni x\mapsto(n,x)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
for all
n
in
{\rm Nature}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

The map
\hat{i}_{X}:\hat{X}_{h}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})
is a coarse embedding.

We now assume that
f:X\to Y
is a uniform covering. It induces
map of sets

{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
given by
(t,x)\mapsto(t,f(x))
.

Proposition 3.11
.

The map
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
is a branched coarse covering with respect to
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
.

Proof.

We verify the conditions listed in
Definition

2.1
.

Definition

2.1
.
1

follows from
Definition

3.1
.
1
and the usual functoriality of the cone
[
BE20b
]
.

Definition

2.1
.
2
follows from
Definition

3.1
.
2
.

We now consider
Definition

2.1
.
3
.
As connection for
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)
we take
\hat{P}:=({\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}})\times P
.
For an entourage
U
of
Y
we set
\tilde{U}:=P\cap f^{-1}(U)
.

We start with checking
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
.
Let
V
be a coarse entourage of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
. Let
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
be an entourage as in
Definition

3.1
.
3b
.
We can find
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that if
((t^{\prime},y^{\prime}),(t,y))
is in
V
and
t\geq n
, then
t^{\prime}\geq n-1
and
(y^{\prime},y)\in U
.

Let now
(t,y)
be in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
,
t\geq n
, and
(t,x)
be in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}(\{(t,y)\})
. Consider furthermore a point
((t^{\prime},y^{\prime}),(t,y))
in
V
.
Then there is a unique
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})
in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}(t^{\prime},y^{\prime})

such that
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in\hat{P}
. Indeed, we must take for
x^{\prime}
the uniquely determined element in
\tilde{U}[x]
over
y^{\prime}
in
U[y]
.

We now check
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)ii
.
We must check that

\hat{P}\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)^{c}_{\leq n})
is a coarse entourage of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
. First of all
Definition

3.1
.
3a
implies that

\hat{P}\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)^{c}_{\leq n})
is a coarse entourage of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes X
. We must further show that
if
\hat{U}
is in
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
and
\epsilon
is in
(0,\infty)
, then there exists
n^{\prime}
in
{\rm Nature}
such that for
((t^{\prime},x^{\prime}),(t,x))\in\hat{P}\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)^{c}_{\leq n})
with
t\geq n^{\prime}
implies
|t^{\prime}-t|<\epsilon
and
(x^{\prime},x)\in\hat{U}
.

First of all by
Definition

3.1
.
3c

there exists
U^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{Y}
such that
U^{\prime}\subseteq U

and
\tilde{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\hat{U}
. There exists
n^{\prime}
in
{\rm Nature}

such that
n\leq n^{\prime}
and
((t^{\prime},y^{\prime}),(t,y))\in V
with
t\geq n^{\prime}
implies that

(y^{\prime},y)\in U^{\prime}
and
|t^{\prime}-t|<\epsilon
. Hence if
((t^{\prime},x^{\prime}),(t,x))\in\hat{P}\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)^{c}_{\leq n})
such that
t\geq n^{\prime}
, then also
(x^{\prime},x)\in\tilde{U}^{\prime}\subseteq\hat{U}
.

We finally check
Definition

2.1
.
3b
.
Let
\hat{V}
be a coarse entourage of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
. Then we must show that
there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that

\hat{V}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)^{c}_{\leq n}}\subseteq\hat{P}
. By

Definition

3.1
.
3c

there exists a uniform entourage
\hat{U}
of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes X
contained in
\hat{P}
. In view of the definition of the hybrid structure there exists
n
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
\hat{V}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)^{c}_{\leq n}}\subseteq\hat{U}
.
Hence
\hat{V}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)^{c}_{\leq n}}\subseteq\hat{P}
as required.
∎

Corollary 3.12
.

1.

If
f
is a
G
-equivariant uniform covering, then
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
is a
G
-equivariant branced coarse
G
-covering with respect to
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
.

2.

If
f:X\to Y
is a uniform
G
-covering, then
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
is a branced coarse
G
-covering with respect to
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
.

To a bornological coarse space
X
with entourage
U
we associate the Rips complex
P_{U}(X)

which we consider as a uniform bornological coarse space. If
X^{\prime}
and
U^{\prime}
is a second bornological coarse space
X
with entourage
U^{\prime}
and
f:X\to X^{\prime}
is a map
f(U)\subseteq U^{\prime}
, then we get an induced map

P(f):P_{U}(X)\to P_{U^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
.
If
f
is proper or bornological, respectively, then
P(f)
has the same property.

We now assume that
f:X\to Y
is a branched coarse covering with respect to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
in
Y
.

Proposition 3.13
.

For every
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}

there exists a member
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
P(f_{|f^{-1}(Z^{c})}):P_{U}(f^{-1}(Z^{c}))\to P_{f(U)}(Z^{c})

is a uniform covering.

Proof.

The map
P(f):P_{U}(X)\to P_{f(U)}(Y)
is
1
-Lipschitz and therefore controlled and uniform. Hence

Definition

3.1
.
1
is satisfied.

Since
f
is bornological, also
P_{U}(f)
is bornological.

We choose
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that the parallel transport for
f
at scale
f(U)^{2}
is defined on
Z^{c}
.
For a simplex in
P_{f(U)}(Z^{c})
the choice of a preimage of a vertex uniquely determines a preimage of
the simplex.

The preimage of a point in a simplex of
P_{f(U)}(Z^{c})
is in bijection to the fibre of
f

on a vertex of this simplex by a map of propagation
1
. This implies that the fibres of
f
are locally finite.
Hence

Definition

3.1
.
2
is satisfied.

We now argue that
Definition

3.1
.
3
is satisfied.
For the connection of
P(f_{|Z^{c}})
we can take the metric entourage of width
1
.
For the entourage
U
in
Definition

3.1
.
3b
we can also take the metric entourage of width
1
.
∎

Assume now that
f:X\to Y
is a
G
-equivariant branched coarse covering with respect to the invariant big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
of
Y
.
If
U
is a
G
-invariant entourage of
X
, then
P(f):P_{U}(X)\to P_{f(U)}(Y)
is clearly
G
-invariant.
The first assertion of the following corollary follows from the assertion of
Proposition

3.13

while the second uses details of the proof.

Corollary 3.14
.

1.

For every
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}^{G}

there exists a member
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
P(f_{|f^{-1}(Z^{c})}):P_{U}(f^{-1}(Z^{c}))\to P_{f(U)}(Z^{c})

is a
G
-equivariant uniform covering.

2.

f
is a branched coarse
G
-covering with respect to
{\mathcal{Z}}
, then with
U
and
Z
as above

P(f_{|f^{-1}(Z^{c})}):P_{U}(f^{-1}(Z^{c}))\to P_{f(U)}(Z^{c})

is a uniform
G
-covering.

4
Finite asymptotic dimension

We start with some notions associated with a family

{\mathcal{W}}
of subsets of a set
X
.
If
U
is an an entourage of
X
and
B
is a subset, then we say that
B
is

U
-bounded if
B\times B\subseteq U
.
The entourage
U
is called
a Lebesgue entourage of
{\mathcal{W}}
if every
U
-bounded subset of
X
is contained in a member of
{\mathcal{W}}
.
We let

\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}}):=\{U\subseteq X\times X\mid\mbox{$U$ is Lebesgue entourage of ${\mathcal{W}}$}\}

denote the set of all Lebesgue entourages of
{\mathcal{W}}
.
The bound of
{\mathcal{W}}
is the entourage

\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}}):=\bigcup_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}}W\times W

of
X
.
Finally, the
multiplicity of the family
{\mathcal{W}}
is defined as

\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}}):=\sup_{x\in X}|\{W\in{\mathcal{W}}\mid x\in W\}|\ .

Note that
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})
is an element of
{\rm Nature}\cup\infty
.

We now assume that
X
is a coarse space with coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
.

Definition 4.1
.

1.

X
has dimension
n
at scale
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
if there exists a family
{\mathcal{W}}
with
U\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
,
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
, and
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})\leq n+1
.

2.

X
has finite dimension at coarse scales if
it has finite dimension at every scale
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
(the dimension may depend on the scale).

3.

X

has asymptotic dimension
\leq n
if it has dimension
\leq n
at every scale
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a big family on
X
, then we say that
(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
has a property listed in
Definition

4.1
eventually
if there exists a member
Y
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
Y^{c}
has this property. Thereby in
Item

2
the set
Y
may depend on
U
.

Construction 4.2
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a coarse branched covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
on
Y
and assume that
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
has
eventually finite dimension at coarse scales. Let
P
denote a connection for
f
.
Let
V
be an entourage of
Y
. Then we can choose a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
as follows:

1.

We first choose
Z
such that
U:=f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})\cap P
is a coarse entourage of
X
. Here we apply
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)ii
.

2.

After increasing
Z
if necessary there exists a covering
{\mathcal{W}}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}
of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
with Lebesgue entourage
U^{2}
,
\tilde{U}:=\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
, and with multiplicity
n:=\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})<\infty
. This uses the assumption.

3.

We increase
Z
further in order to ensure that restriction of
f
to any
\tilde{U}
-bounded subset of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})

is injective. To this end by
Definition

2.1
.
3(a)i
we must choose
Z
so large that the parallel transport at scale
f(\tilde{U})
is defined on
Z^{c}
. We then restrict the covering
{\mathcal{W}}
to the preimage of the new
Z^{c}
.

Recall the definition

U(B):=\{x\in X\mid U[x]\subseteq B\}
of the
U
-thinning of a subset
B
of
X
.
Since
U^{2}
is a Lebesgue entourage of
{\mathcal{W}}
the
family of
U
-thinnings
(U(W_{i}))_{i\in I}
is still a covering.
By choosing appropriate subsets
\hat{W}_{i}\subseteq U(W_{i})
for all
i
in
I
we can construct a partition

\hat{\mathcal{W}}:=(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
.
We have
U[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
for every
i
in
I
.

If
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has finite asymptotic dimension
\leq n^{\prime}
, then we can take
n=n^{\prime}+1
independently of
V
.

∎

Construction 4.3
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a coarse branched covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
on
Y
and assume that
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
has
eventually finite dimension at coarse scales. We choose a connection
P
for
f
. Le
V
be an entourage of
Y
. Then we can choose a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
as follows:

1.

Using the assumption we first choose
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that there exists a covering
{\mathcal{W}}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}

of
Z^{c}
with Lebesgue entourage
V^{2}
,
\tilde{V}:=\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}
, and
n:=\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})<\infty
.

2.

Using
Definition

2.1
.
3a
we then enlarge
Z
such that the parallel transport at scale
\tilde{V}
is defined on
Z^{c}
and

f^{-1}(\tilde{V}_{Z^{c}})\cap P
is a coarse entourage of
X
.
We restrict
{\mathcal{W}}
to the new
Z^{c}
.

Then
(V(W_{i}))_{i\in I}
is still a covering of
Z^{c}
.
By choosing appropriate subsets
\hat{W}_{i}\subseteq V(W_{i})
for all
i
in
I
we can construct a partition

\hat{\mathcal{W}}:=(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
of
Z^{c}
. We have
V[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
for every
i
in
I
.

If
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
eventually has finite asymptotic dimension
\leq n^{\prime}
, then we can take
n=n^{\prime}+1
independently of
V
.

∎

Let
f:X\to Y
be a coarse branched covering relative to the family
{\mathcal{Z}}
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

If
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
has eventually finite asymptotic dimension
\leq n
(finite dimension at coarse scales), then

(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
also has eventually finite asymptotic dimension
\leq n
(finite dimension at coarse scales).

Proof.

It suffices to show for every
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
, that if
Y
eventually has dimension
n
at scale
V:=f(U)
, then

X
has eventually dimension
n
at scale
U
.
We can assume that
U
and hence
V
are symmetric.

We choose a connection
P
.
By assumption we can choose
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
and a covering
{\mathcal{W}}
of
Z^{c}
with Lebesgue entourage
V
, bound
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
and
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})\leq n+1
such that the parallel transport is defined at scales
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})
,
V\circ\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})
and
V
on
Z^{c}
, and that
U_{f^{-1}(Z^{c})}\subseteq P
.

Without loss of generality we can assume that all members of
{\mathcal{W}}
are non-empty. For every
W
in
{\mathcal{W}}
we choose a base point
y_{W}
. Then we define the family of subsets

\tilde{\mathcal{W}}:=((\tilde{W}_{x})_{x\in f^{-1}(y_{W})})_{W\in{\mathcal{W}}}
of
X
, where
\tilde{W}_{x}:=\{\Phi_{y^{\prime},y_{W}}(x)\mid y^{\prime}\in W\}
, the set of all parallel transports
of points
x
in
f^{-1}(y_{W})
to points in fibres of points in
W
.

We claim that
\tilde{W}
does the job.
We first check that
U_{f^{-1}(Z^{c})}
is a Lebesgue entourage of
\tilde{\mathcal{W}}
. Thus let
A
be a
U_{f^{-1}(Z^{c})}
-bounded subset of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
. Then
f(A)
is
V
-bounded. Hence there exists some
W
in
{\mathcal{W}}
such that
f(A)\subseteq W
.
Let
a_{0}
be in
A
. Then
f(a_{0})\in W
and since the parallel transport is defined at scale
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})
on
Z^{c}
there exists a unique
x
in
f^{-1}(y_{W})
such that
(a,x)\in P
.
We claim that
A\subseteq\tilde{W}_{x}
.
Let
a
be any other point in
A
. Then
(a,a_{0})\in U_{f^{-1}(Z^{c})}\subseteq P
. Since
f(A)
is

V
-bounded we know that
a
is the parallel transport of
a_{0}
. Since the parallel transport on
Z^{c}
is even defined at scale
V\circ\mathrm{bd}(W)
we know that
a
is the parallel transport of
y_{W}
. Hence
a\in\tilde{W}_{x}
, too.

Let
x_{0}
be in
X
. If
W
in
{\mathcal{W}}
is such that
f(x_{0})\in W
, then
there exists a unique
x
in
f^{-1}(y_{W})
with
x_{0}\in\tilde{W}_{x}
. Indeed,
x
must be the parallel transport at scale
V
(at this point we use the assumption that
V
is symmetric) of

x_{0}
to the fibre
f^{-1}(y_{W})
.
This observation implies that
\mathrm{mult}(\tilde{\mathcal{W}})\leq\mathrm{mult}(W)\leq n+1
.
∎

In the following we propose the analogue of the notion of topological dimension
\leq n
in the context of uniform
spaces. Recall that a topological space
X
has topological dimension
\leq n
if every covering
{\mathcal{W}}
of
X
has a refinement

{\mathcal{V}}
with
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{V}})\leq n+1
. In the context of uniform spaces it is natural to add a condition on the lebesgue
entourages of
{\mathcal{V}}
in order to ensure that
{\mathcal{V}}
does not become too small.
The following definition is designed such the cone functor and the squeezing space functor
translate finite uniform topological dimension into finite asymptotic dimension, see
Corollary

4.10
.

Let
X
be a uniform space with uniform structure
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
.

Definition 4.5
.

X
has uniform topological dimension
\leq n
(finite uniform topological dimension at uniform scales) if
there exists a cofinal function
\kappa:{\mathcal{U}}_{X}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
such that
for every
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
there exists a covering
{\mathcal{W}}
of
X
with

U\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
,
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\subseteq\kappa(U)
and
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})\leq n+1
(
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})<\infty
).

We write
\mathrm{udim}(X)\leq n
if
X
has uniform topological dimension
\leq n
.

Remark 4.6
.

If the uniform structure on
X
is induced by a metric and
then function
\kappa
is required to be linear in the metric scale, then

Definition

4.5
reduces the definition of finite Assouad–Nagata dimension.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

If
X
is an
n
-dimensional simplicial complex with the spherical path metric, then
X

has uniform topological dimension
\leq n
.

Proof.

For
n
in
{\rm Nature}

we let
{\mathcal{W}}_{n}
be the covering of
X
by the stars of the vertices in the
n
th barycentric subdivision.
We set furthermore
{\mathcal{W}}_{-1}:=(X)
.
Then
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}}_{n})\leq\dim(X)+1
for all
n\in{\rm Nature}\cup\{-1\}
.
We define
s:{\mathcal{U}}_{X}\to{\rm Nature}\cup\{-1\}

by

s(U):=\max\{n\in{\rm Nature}\cup\{-1\}\mid U\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}}_{n})\}\ .

For every
U
in
{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
we can then take the covering
{\mathcal{W}}_{s(U)}
.
We observe that

\kappa:{\mathcal{U}}_{X}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
given by
U\mapsto\mathrm{bd}(W_{s(U)})
is cofinal.
∎

Note that smooth manifolds admit triangulations.

Corollary 4.8
.

A closed Riemannian manifold
M
with the metric uniform structure
has uniform topological dimension
\leq\dim(M)
.

For complete manifolds must add bounded geometry assumptions which imply that
the uniform structure given by the Riemannian metric is compatible with the spherical path metric coming from a triangulation.

Let
X
be a uniform bornological coarse space with a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}=(Y_{i})_{i\in I}
.
We let
X_{h}
denote the bornological coarse space obtained from
X
by replacing the coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}

by the hybrid coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}_{X_{h}}
associated to
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proposition 4.9
.

1.

We assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}=(Y_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
, i.e., that the big family is indexed by natural numbers.

2.

We assume that the uniform structure of
X
admits a countable cofinal family of entourages.

3.

We assume that there exists a uniform entourage
U_{0}
such that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
U_{0}
-increasing in the sense that there exists
n_{0}
in
{\rm Nature}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
we have
U_{0}[Y_{n}^{c}]\subseteq Y_{n-1}^{c}
.

4.

We assume that
X
has finite (coarse) asymptotic dimension.

5.

We assume that
X
has finite uniform topological dimension.

Then

X_{h}
has finite asymptotic dimension.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathrm{adim}(X)\leq n^{\prime}-1
and
\mathrm{udim}(X)\leq n^{\prime\prime}-1
. The assumptions
4.9
.
1
and
4.9
.
2
on
X
imply that every hybrid entourage in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X_{h}}
is contained in an entourage of the form
U\cap U_{\delta}
, where
U
is in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
and

\delta:{\rm Nature}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
is a cofinal function determining

U_{\delta}:=\{(x,y)\in X\times X\mid\forall n\in{\rm Nature}:x\in Y_{n}^{c}\lor y\in Y_{n}^{c}\implies(x,y)\in\delta(n)\}\ .

We will construct a covering
{\mathcal{W}}
with
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})\leq 3n^{\prime}+2n^{\prime\prime}
,

\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X_{h}}
and
U\cap U_{\delta}\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
.
Since
\mathrm{adim}(X)\leq n^{\prime}-1
we can find a covering
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
with
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
,
U\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})

and
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})\leq n^{\prime}
.

Let
\kappa:{\mathcal{U}}_{X}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
be as in
Definition

4.5
.
For every
n
in
{\rm Nature}
we can choose a covering
{\mathcal{W}}_{n}
of
X
with

\delta(n)\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
,
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\subseteq\kappa(\delta(n))
and
\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}}_{n})\leq n^{\prime\prime}
.

By cofinality of
\delta
there exists
n_{1}\geq n_{0}
in
{\rm Nature}
such that
\delta(n_{1})\subseteq U_{0}
.
We then define the covering

{\mathcal{W}}:=({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap Y_{n_{1}+1})\cup\bigcup_{n\geq n_{1}+1}((Y_{n+1}\setminus Y_{n-1})\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{\delta(n)})\ .

(4.1)

We show that
U\cap U_{\delta}\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
.
Let
B
be
U\cap U_{\delta}
-bounded.
If
B\subseteq Y_{n_{1}+1}
, then since
B
is
U
-bounded there exists

W
in
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
with
B\subseteq W
.
Hence

B\subseteq Y_{n_{1}+1}\cap W
and therefore
B
is contained in a member of
{\mathcal{W}}
.
Since
B
is coarsely bounded, if
B\not\subseteq Y_{n_{1}+1}
, then there exists a minimal
n>n_{1}+1
such that

B\subseteq Y_{n+1}
. Minimality of
n
implies that
B\cap Y_{n}^{c}\not=\emptyset
.
Since
B
is
U_{\delta}
-bounded we get
B\subseteq\delta(n)[Y_{n}^{c}]\subseteq U_{0}[Y_{n}^{c}]\subseteq Y_{n-1}^{c}
. Hence
B\subseteq Y_{n+1}\setminus Y_{n-1}
.
Since
B
is
U_{\delta}
-bounded it is
\delta(n)
-bounded and there exists
W
in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\delta(n)}

a
such that
B\subseteq W
. Since
B
is
U
-bounded there exists furthermore
W^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
with
B\subseteq W^{\prime}
. Consequently,
B\subseteq(Y_{n+1}\setminus Y_{n-1})\cap W\cap W^{\prime}

and is therefore contained in a member of
{\mathcal{W}}
.
This finishes the verification of
U\cap U_{\delta}\in\mathrm{leb}({\mathcal{W}})
.

By an inspection of (
4.1
) we get
the crude estimate

\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})\leq 3n^{\prime}+2n^{\prime\prime}
.
We define the cofinal function
\delta^{\prime}:{\rm Nature}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{X}
by

\delta^{\prime}(n):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}X\times X&n\leq n_{1}+1\\
\kappa(\delta(n-1))&n>n_{1}+1\end{array}\right.\ .

Then again by an inspection of (
4.1
) we get
\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\subseteq\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})\cap U_{\delta^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{X_{h}}
.
∎

Let
X
be a uniform bornological coarse space. Recall the
Definition

3.6
of the squeezing space
\hat{X}_{h}
and of the
Construction

3.9
of the cone
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
.

Corollary 4.10
.

Assume that the uniform structure of
X
has countably generated.
If
X
has finite (coarse) asymptotic dimension and finite uniform topological dimension, then
\hat{X}_{h}

and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
have
finite asymptotic dimension.

5
Coarse homology theories and transfers

In this section we axiomatically introduce the concept of transfers along coarse branched coverings for equivariant coarse homology theories. We build on the axioms for a equivariant coarse homology theory proposed in
[
BEKW20a
]
.
Transfers along bounded coarse coverings have been introduced in
[
BEKW20c
]
, see
Lemma

2.4
.
In this section we generalize this concept to branched coarse coverings.
In order to capture the complete functoriality of transfers for bounded coarse coverings in
[
BEKW20c
]

we introduced a
(2,1)
-category
G\mathbf{BC}_{\mathrm{tr}}
of spans of bornological coarse spaces whose wrong-way directions consisted of
bounded coarse coverings. In the present paper we will not consider the full possible functoriality of transfers for branched coarse coverings. In particular we will not discuss their composition.
We can therefore work with the one-categories
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
and
G\mathbf{BrCov}
which we will introduce below.

We let
G\mathbf{BC}^{2}
be the category of pairs in
G\mathbf{BC}
defined as follows:

1.

objects: The objects of
G\mathbf{BC}^{2}
are pairs
(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
of
X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
and an invariant big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
on
X
.

2.

morphisms: A morphism
g:(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\to(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
G\mathbf{BC}^{2}
is a morphism
g:X^{\prime}\to X
in
G\mathbf{BC}
such that
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\subseteq g^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

3.

composition: The composition is inherited from the composition in
G\mathbf{BC}
.

We have a natural inclusion
G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{BC}^{2}
sending
X
to
(X,(\emptyset))
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.
Any functor
E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
extends to a functor

E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}^{2}\to{\mathcal{C}}
given by

E^{G}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}):=\mathrm{Cofib}(E^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})\to E^{G}(X))\ .

(5.1)

By definition we have a natural fibre sequence

E^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})\to E^{G}(X)\to E^{G}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\to\Sigma E^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

We define the category
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
of
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings as follows:

1.

The objects of
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
are pairs
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
of a
G
-equivariant branched coarse covering

f:X\to Y
relative to the invariant big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
on
Y
.

2.

A morphism
(g,h):(f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\to(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
is a pair of morphisms
g,h
in
G\mathbf{BC}
such that

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y}

(5.2)

is a cartesian diagram of coarse spaces, the bornology of
X^{\prime}
is induced via
g
from the bornology of
X
, and
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}\subseteq f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}})
(see
Lemma

2.17
).

3.

composition: The composition is inherited from the composition in
G\mathbf{BC}
.

Sometimes will will consider full subcategories of
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
defined by putting additional conditions on the objects, e.g. the condition of having eventually finite asymptotic dimension, which will be indicated by a superscript like in
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{\mathrm{fadim}}
.

We let

G\mathbf{BrCov}
be the full subcategory of
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
of branched coarse
G
-coverings.

We have forgetful functors

s:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}\to G\mathbf{BC}^{2}\ ,\quad t:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}\to G\mathbf{BC}^{2}

(source and target) given by
s(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y,{\mathcal{Z}}):=(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))

and
t(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y,{\mathcal{Z}}):=(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
. The description of these functors on morphisms is the obvious one.
Furthermore, the restriction of
t
of
G\mathbf{BrCov}
factorizes over a functor

G\mathbf{BrCov}\to\mathbf{BC}^{2}
which we will also denote by
t
.

We now consider a functor

E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

Then we can define the functors

t^{*}E^{G},s^{*}E^{G}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

Let
C
be a condition on the objects of
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
and

{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{C}
be a full subcategory of
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings
satisfying
C
.

Definition 5.1
.

We say that
E^{G}
has transfers for
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings satisfying condition
C

if
E^{G}
is equipped with the additional structure of a natural transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}E^{G}\to s^{*}E^{G}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{C}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

We write
\mathrm{tr}_{f}
for the evaluation of
\mathrm{tr}
at the
G
-equivariant branched coarse covering
f
.

Example 5.2
.

If
E^{G}
has transfers for bounded coarse coverings
in the sense of
[
BEKW20c
]
, then it has transfers
for
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
satisfying the condition

C=\mbox{$f$ is bounded coarse covering}\ ,

see

Lemma

2.4
.
∎

We now consider a pair of an equivariant coarse homology theory
E^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

and a coarse homology theory
E:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 5.3
.

We say that
E^{G}
and
E
are related by a transfer (equivalence) for branched coarse
G
-coverings satisfying condition
C

if we have the additional structure of a natural transformation (equivalence)

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}E\to s^{*}E^{G}:G\mathbf{BrCov}^{C}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

We again write
\mathrm{tr}_{f}
for the evaluation of
\mathrm{tr}
at the branched coarse
G
-covering
f
.

Example 5.4
.

We can define

E(-):=E^{G}(G_{min,min}\otimes\mathrm{Res}_{G}(-)):\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

To give a transfer equivalence relating

E^{G}
and
E
for branched coarse
G
-coverings which are bounded coarse coverings and therefore isomorphic to

G_{min,min}\otimes X\to X
is equivalent to give a trivialization of the
G
-action on
the functor
E^{G}(G_{min,min}\otimes-)
induced by the right
G
-action on
G_{min,min}
.
∎

We define the category
{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}
of
G
-equivariant uniform coverings as follows:

1.

The objects of
{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}
are
G
-equivariant uniform coverings
f:X\to Y
.

2.

A morphism
(g,h):(f:X\to Y)\to(f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime})
is a pair of morphisms

g,h
in
\mathbf{UBC}
such that

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y}

is a cartesian square of uniform and coarse spaces and the bornology of
X^{\prime}
is induced via
g
from the bornology of
X
.

We let
G\mathbf{UCov}
denote the full subcategory of
{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}
of uniform
G
-coverings.

The cone functor
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}
induces a functor

{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}\to{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}

which sends

(f:X\to Y)
to
({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y))
.
These constructions preserve
G
-coverings.

Note that the transfer for a branched coarse covering is defined one the level of relative homology.
Taking advantage of the fact that the members of the big family
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
are flasque
for cones of uniform coverings we can define an absolute transfer.

Let
C
be a condition ensuring that
E^{G}
has transfers for
G
-equivariant coarse coverings
satisfying
C
. We let
{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}^{C}
be the full subcategory of
G
-equivariant uniform coverings
f:X\to Y

such that
({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f):{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y))\in{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{C}
.
See e.g.
Corollary

4.10
for conditions involving dimensions.

The horizontal maps in the square below are equivalences since
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y)
consist of flasque members.

Definition 5.5
.

We define the cone transfer

\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}}:t^{*}E^{G}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\to s^{*}E^{G}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}^{C}\to{\mathcal{C}}

such that

\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X))}

naturally commutes.

We let
G\mathbf{UCov}^{C}
be the full subcategory of
{}^{G}\mathbf{UCov}
of uniform
G
-coverings.
We furthermore consider a pair
(E^{G},E)
of homology theories related by a transfer (equivalence) for branched coarse
G
-coverings
satisfying condition
C
(see
Definition

5.3
).

Definition 5.6
.

We define the cone transfer (equivalence)

\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}}:t^{*}E{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}\to s^{*}E^{G}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:G\mathbf{UCov}^{C}\to{\mathcal{C}}

such that

\textstyle{E({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\textstyle{E({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{E^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X))}

naturally commutes.

6
The transfer for coarse algebraic
K
-homology

In this section we show that the equivariant coarse algebraic
K
-theory functor
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
with coefficients in an additive category
{\mathbf{A}}
with strict
G
-action
admits the additional structure of transfers for all
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings.
We will construct this structure on the level of additive categories of equivariant controlled objects in
{\mathbf{A}}
.
We choose an approach which can easily be modified to cover the case of topological coarse
K
-homology in subsequent sections.

Let
k
be a commutative base ring.
We consider an idempotent complete and sum complete compactly generated
k
-linear additive category
{\mathbf{A}}
with
G
-action.
We write
M\to gM
and
A\mapsto gA
for the action of
g
in
G
on objects and morphisms of
{\mathbf{A}}
.

To a
G
-bornological coarse space
X
we associate the
k
-linear additive category
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
of
X
-controlled objects
in
{\mathbf{A}}
which we describe in the following.

1.

The objects of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
are locally finite equivariant
X
-controlled objects
(M,\rho,\mu)
where:

(a)

M
is an object of
{\mathbf{A}}
.

(b)

\rho=(\rho_{g})_{g\in G}
is a family of isomorphisms
\rho_{g}:M\to gM
satisfying the cocycle condition

g\rho_{g^{\prime}}\circ\rho_{g}=\rho_{g^{\prime}g}
for all
g,g^{\prime}
in
G
.

(c)

\mu
is an equivariant finitely additive locally finite projection-valued measure on
M
defined on the power set
\mathcal{P}(X)
of
X
. Equivariance is the condition
\mu(gY)=\rho_{g}^{-1}g\mu(Y)\rho_{g}
for all
Y
in
\mathcal{P}(X)
and
g
in
G
. Local finiteness is the condition that there exists a family

(M(x),u(x))_{x\in X}
of compact objects
M(x)
of
{\mathbf{A}}
and morphisms
u(x):M(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}M

presenting
M
as the direct sum of the family
(M(x))_{x\in X}

such that for every bounded set
B
of
X
the set
\{x\in B\mid M(x)\not=0\}
is finite and for every
Y
in
\mathcal{P}(X)

we have

\mu(Y)u(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}u(x)&x\in Y\\
0&x\not\in Y\end{array}\right.\ .

(6.1)

2.

Morphisms in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
are equivariant and controlled morphisms in
{\mathbf{A}}
. More precisely, a morphism
A:(M,\rho,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
is a morphism
A:M\to M^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{A}}

which satisfies
gA=\rho_{g}A\rho_{g}^{-1}
for all
g
in
G
, and which is
U
-controlled for some
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{X}
in the sense that
\mu^{\prime}(B^{\prime})A\mu(B)=0
whenever
B,B^{\prime}
are in
\mathcal{P}(X)
and
B^{\prime}\cap U[B]=\emptyset
.

3.

The
k
-linear structure and the composition is inherited from
{\mathbf{A}}
.

In the notation of
Item

1c
for every
x
in
X
we define a morphism

u(x)^{*}:M\to M(x)
(the projection onto the summand
M(x)
) by the condition that

u(x)^{*}u(x^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathrm{id}_{M(x)}&x=x^{\prime}\\
0&x\not=x^{\prime}\end{array}\right.\ .

(6.2)

A morphism
A:M\to M^{\prime}
is completely determined by its matrix coefficients

A_{x^{\prime},x}:=u^{\prime}(x^{\prime})^{*}Au(x):M(x)\to M^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\ .

The only condition is that for every
x
in
X
the set
\{x^{\prime}\in X\mid A_{x^{\prime},x}\not=0\}
is finite. If
A
is
a morphism in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
, then

{\mathtt{supp}}(A):=\{(x^{\prime},x)\in X\times X\mid A_{x^{\prime},x}\not=0\}

is an invariant coarse entourage of
X
called the propagation of
A
.

For a morphism
f:X\to X^{\prime}
of
G
-bornological coarse space we define the
k
-linear additive functor

f_{*}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X^{\prime})
as follows:

1.

The functor
f_{*}
sends the object
(M,\rho,\mu)
to the object
f_{*}(M,\rho,\mu)=(M,\rho,f_{*}\mu)

with
f_{*}\mu(Y):=\mu(f^{-1}(Y))
for all
Y
in
\mathcal{P}(X^{\prime})
.

2.

It acts as identity on morphisms.

One checks that
f_{*}(M,\rho,\mu)
is again a locally finite equivariant
X^{\prime}
-controlled object.
If
A:(M,\rho,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
, then

A
considered as a morphism
f_{*}(M,\rho,\mu)\to f_{*}(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is still equivariant and controlled, now over
X^{\prime}
.
We thus get a functor

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}

from
G
-bornlogical coarse spaces to
k
-linear additive categories.

The construction is also functorial in the
k
-linear additive category
{\mathbf{A}}
.
We let
G\mathbf{Add}_{k}^{\sharp}
be the subcategory of
G\mathbf{Add}_{k}
of idempotent and sum complete
compactly generated additive categories with
G
-action and equivariant compact object preserving additive functors.

Lemma 6.1
.

The construction
{\mathbf{A}}\mapsto{\mathbf{V}}^{G}(X)
extends to a functor

G\mathbf{Add}_{k}^{\sharp}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}
.

Proof.

This is straightforward.
∎

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an invariant big family in
X
.
For
Y
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
we can regard
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y)
as a full subcategory of

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)
of objects supported on
Y
. Then

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\bigcup_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y)

is a Karoubi filtration
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 8.3]
and we can define the quotient category

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}):=\frac{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)}{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}({\mathcal{Y}})}\ .

In this way we get a functor

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:G\mathbf{BC}^{2}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}\ ,\qquad(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\mapsto{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\ .

We want to construct equivariant coarse homology theories by composing
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
with
functors
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 6.2
.

We say that
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a homological functor for
k
-linear additive categories if it satisfies the following conditions:

1.

{\mathcal{C}}
is a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category.

2.

H
sends equivalences of
k
-linear additive categories to equivalences.

3.

H
preserves filtered colimits.

4.

H
sends Karoubi filtrations to fibre sequences.

5.

H
annihilates flasque objects (see
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 8.1]
).

Example 6.3
.

Here is a list examples of homological functors.

1.

We let
\mathbf{Add}:=\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
denote the category of additive categories.
As explained in
[
BEKW20a
, Sec. 8.1]
(see also
Remark

13.5
) the non-connective algebraic
K
-theory functor

K^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(
13.4
) is a homological functor for additive categories.

2.

[
BC20
]
proposes to consider the functor

\mathrm{UK}:\mathbf{Add}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}

where the second morphism is the universal localizing invariant of

[
BGT13
]
. The properties of
\mathrm{UK}
verified in the reference imply that it is a homological functor.

3.

The
homotopy
K
-theory
K^{\mathrm{alg}}H:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}
for additive categories (
13.36
) is a homological functor by
Corollary

13.22
.

4.

Hochschild homology
HH:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to D({\mathbb{Q}})
or cyclic homology
CH:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to D({\mathbb{Q}})

for
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear additive categories are homological functors, see
[
Cap20
]
for a discussion of these in connection with coarse homology theories.

5.

If
R
is a unital
k
-algebra and
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a homological functor, then we can form a new homological functor

H_{R}(-):=H(R\otimes_{k}-)
. If
R
is non-unital, then we set

H_{R}:={\mathrm{Fib}}(H_{R^{+}}\to H_{k})
, where the map is induced by the canonical projection
R^{+}\to k
from the unitalization
R^{+}
.

6.

We consider the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Add}
.
If
H:\mathbf{Add}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a homological functor for additive categories, then its restriction along
{\mathcal{Z}}
is a homological functor

H{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}

for
k
-linear additive categories.

7.

In the case
k={\mathbb{C}}
the most important example for the present paper is the homological
functor

K^{\mathrm{alg}}H{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
, where

{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
is the algebra of trace class operators on the Hilbert space
\ell^{2}
.
Here we combine the constructions from
Item

5
and
Item

6
.

∎

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be an idempotent complete and sum complete compactly generated
k
-linear additive category
{\mathbf{A}}
with
G
-action.
For a functor
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
we consider the composition

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:=H\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(6.3)

Theorem 6.4
.

If
H
is homological, then

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
is an equivariant coarse homology theory which is in addition
strong and continuous.

Proof.

In
[
BEKW20a
, Sec. 8.3]
this statement was shown for
H=K
. We just observe that the argument
only depends on the properties of the functor
H
listed in
Definition

6.2
.
∎

Using the notation of (
5.1
) we have

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq H({\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}}))\ .

(6.4)

Applying
Theorem

6.4
to the homological functor

K^{\mathrm{alg}}{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}
(see
Item

6
) we get:

Corollary 6.5
.

The functor

K^{\mathrm{alg}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:=K^{\mathrm{alg}}{\mathcal{Z}}\circ{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Sp}

is an equivariant coarse homology theory called the equivariant coarse algebraic
K
-homology with coefficients in
{\mathbf{A}}
. It is strong and continuous.

Note that
K^{\mathrm{alg}}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
is also strongly additive by
[
BEKW20a
, Prop. 8.19]

in the sense that it sends free unions of
G
-bornological coarse spaces to products.

We now start with the construction of transfers.
We let

\ell:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k,2,1}

denote the Dwyer-Kan localization at equivalences.
We realize
\mathbf{Add}_{k,2,1}
by the strict two category of additive categories, additive functors and equivalences.
In the following theorem we consider the functor
\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k,2,1}
.
Going over to this localization is necessary since
the construction of the transfer involves choices which would destroy the one-categorical naturality.

Theorem 6.6
.

There exists a canonical natural transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\to s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k,2,1}\ .

(6.5)

Proof.

First of all, for every
(X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
in
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
we will construct a
k
-linear functor

\mathrm{tr}_{f}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))

by describing its action on objects and morphisms.
Assume that

(M,\rho,\mu)
is an object in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y)
. Then by
Item

1c
we can choose
a presentation
(M(y),u(y))_{y\in Y}
of
M
as a sum of
the components associated to the points of
Y
.

We then define the object

\mathrm{tr}_{f}(M,\rho,\mu):=(N,\sigma,\nu)

in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
such that

(N,(v(x))_{x\in X})
is a choice of a sum of the family

(M(f(x)))_{x\in X}
. The families
(gM(y),gu(y))_{y\in Y}
and
(gM(f(x)),gv(x))_{x\in X}
present
gM
and
gN
as sums.
The cocycle

\sigma=(\sigma_{g})_{g\in G}
is defined such that

\sigma_{g}v(x)=gv(g^{-1}x)\ gu(g^{-1}x)^{*}\rho_{g}\ u(f(x)):M(f(x))\to gN

for all
x
in
X
.
Finally, the projection valued measure is determined in analogy to (
6.1
) by the condition

\nu(W)v(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}v(x)&x\in W\\
0&x\not\in W\end{array}\right.\ .

(6.6)

One checks that
\mathrm{tr}_{f}(M,\rho,\mu)
is an object of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
.
In particular, in order to verify local finiteness one uses
Definition

2.1
.
2
.

Let
[A]:(M,\rho,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
represented by
a
V
-controlled morphism
A
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y)

for some
V
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}
. We let
P
denote a connection for
f
and choose
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
for
V

as in
Definition

2.1
.
3
.

Since
[A]=[\mu^{\prime}(Z^{c})A\mu(Z^{c})]

we can assume that
A
is supported on
Z^{c}
.
Let
(N,\sigma,\nu)
and
(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
be the transfers of
(M,\rho,\mu)
and
(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.
We then define

\mathrm{tr}_{f}(A):=B:(N,\sigma,\nu)\to(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
such that
its matrix is given by

B_{x^{\prime},x}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}A_{f(x^{\prime}),f(x)}&(f(x^{\prime}),f(x))\in V_{Z^{c}}\>\&\>(x^{\prime},x)\in P\\
0&else\end{array}\right.

(6.7)

from

M(f(x))
to
M(f(x^{\prime}))
. So

B_{x^{\prime},x}\not=0
implies that
x^{\prime}
is the parallel transport of
x
along the one-step
V
-path
(f(x),f(x^{\prime}))
.

One checks that
B
is
f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})\cap P
-controlled and equivariant. Furthermore, the class
[B]
in

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
only depends on the class
[A]
. One finally checks that
this construction is compatible with the composition since path-lifting is compatible with composition of paths. This finishes the construction of the functor
\mathrm{tr}_{f}
.
Note that it depends on the choice of sums (which come together with the families of canonical inclusions).
Different choices lead to canonically isomorphic functors. This will be employed in the following construction.

(6.10)

is a second morphism, then the composition

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{f^{\prime\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{*}}

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{f}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{t_{\Psi}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{*}}
\scriptstyle{t_{\Phi}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)}

is the transformation associated to
\Phi\circ\Psi
.

Saying that an object
R
of
{\mathbf{A}}
is presented as the sum of a family
(R_{i})_{i\in I}
of objects means that we are given
the family of structure morphisms
(R_{i}\to R)_{i\in I}
.
In the following argument we will not write these structure morphisms explicitly
but one must keep track of them in the background.
If
(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y^{\prime})
and
M^{\prime}
is represented as the sum of

(M^{\prime}(y^{\prime}))_{y^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}}
, then writing
h_{*}(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})=(M,\mu,\rho)
we see that
M=M^{\prime}
is canonically isomorphic to the sum of the family
((M^{\prime}(y^{\prime}))_{y^{\prime}\in h^{-1}(y)})_{y\in Y}
. In a first step we
form the partial sums
\bigoplus_{y^{\prime}\in h^{-1}(y)}M^{\prime}(y^{\prime})\cong M(y)
and in a second step we get an isomorphism

\bigoplus_{y\in Y}M(y)\cong M
.

We get

\mathrm{tr}_{f}h_{*}(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})=(N,\sigma,\nu)
, where
N
is represents the sum of the family

((M^{\prime}(y^{\prime}))_{y^{\prime}\in h^{-1}(f(x))})_{x\in X}\ .

The index family can be reordered as
((M^{\prime}(f(x^{\prime})))_{x^{\prime}\in g^{-1}(x)})_{x\in X}
whose sum is
the object appearing in
g_{*}\mathrm{tr}_{f^{\prime}}(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})=(\tilde{N},\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\nu})
. We let
t_{\Phi}:\tilde{N}\to N
denote the corresponding isomorphism.
One then checks the compatibility with morphisms (naturality) and compositions.
∎

Since a homological functor
H
for
k
-linear additive categories sends equivalences to equivalences
it has a factorization over
\ell
as in

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.19614pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.19614pt\raise 51.30743pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{H}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
