We conclude an equivalence of functors

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\simeq H_{2,1}\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(6.11)

Therefore
Theorem

6.6
immediately implies:

Theorem 6.7
.

The equivariant coarse homology functor
H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
admits the structure of transfers

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\to s^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(6.12)

for all
G
-equivariant
coarse branched coverings.

Remark 6.8
.

It follows from an inspection of the internal details of the construction that the transfer for
H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
for
G
-equivariant branched
coarse coverings extends the transfer for bounded coarse coverings constructed in
[
BEKW20c
]
.
∎

We now assume that
{\mathbf{A}}
has the trivial
G
-action.
We then consider the functors

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k}\ .

Theorem 6.9
.

The natural transformation from (
6.5
) restricts to a
natural equivalence

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BrCov}\to\mathbf{Add}_{k,2,1}\ .

Proof.

We reuse the argument for

Theorem

6.6
. Since
G
acts trivially on
{\mathbf{A}}
the cocycles in the controlled objects become actions of
G
on the corresponding objects.
We can interpret
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)
as the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(Y)
of
objects
(M,\rho,\mu)
where
\rho
is the trivial
G
-action.
For varying
Y
we interpret
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
as a subfunctor of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}
.
We then define

\mathrm{tr}:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\to{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}

as the restriction of the transfer constructed in
Theorem

6.6
to this subfunctor.
For morphisms
\Phi
in
G\mathbf{BrCov}
as in (
6.8
) we furthermore define the transformations

t_{\Phi}:g_{*}\mathrm{tr}_{f^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{tr}_{f}h_{*}:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))

by restricting the transformation

t_{\Phi}
constructed in the proof of in
Theorem

6.6
.
In this way we obtain the desired natural transformation.
It remains to show that it is an equivalence.

To this end, for every branched coarse
G
-covering
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
relative to
{\mathcal{Z}}
we construct an inverse functor

r:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\to{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

Let
Z
be in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
G
acts freely and transitively on the fibres of
f
over
Z^{c}
.
Let

(N,\sigma,\nu)
be in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)
. Then
we choose a presentation
(N(x),v(x))_{x\in X}
of
N
as a sum of contributions of the points of
X
.
We choose a set-theoretic section
s:Z^{c}\to f^{-1}(Z^{c})
of
f
.
Then we define
M
as the sum of
(N(s(y)))_{y\in Z^{c}}

and let
u(y):M(s(y))\to M
denote the structure maps.
The projection-valued measure
\mu

on
M
is uniquely characterized by

\mu(W)u(y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}u(y)&y\in W\\
0&y\not\in W\end{array}\right.\ .

(6.13)

In this way we get the object
(M,\mu):=r(N,\sigma,\nu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)
.
Let now

[B]:(N,\sigma,\nu)\to(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
.
We can assume that
B
is supported on
f^{-1}(Z^{\prime,c})
for some
Z^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
Z\subseteq Z^{\prime}
, that there
exists an entourage
V
of
Y
such that
B
is
f^{-1}(V_{Z^{\prime,c}})\cap P
-controlled, and that
the parallel transport at scale
V
is defined on
Z^{\prime,c}
.
For every
y
in
Z^{\prime,c}
and
y^{\prime}
in
Y
with
(y^{\prime},y)\in V
we define
g(y,y^{\prime})
in
G
such that
g(y,y^{\prime})s(y^{\prime})
in
f^{-1}(y^{\prime})
is the parallel transport of
s(y)
along the
V
-path
(y,y^{\prime})
.
We define
A:M\to M^{\prime}
uniquely such that its matrix coefficients are given by

A_{y^{\prime},y}:=\sigma^{\prime}_{g(y^{\prime},y);s^{\prime}(y^{\prime}),g(y^{\prime},y)s^{\prime}(y^{\prime})}B_{g(y^{\prime},y)s^{\prime}(y^{\prime}),s(y)}:M(y)\to M^{\prime}(y^{\prime})\ .

(6.14)

One checks that the class
[A]:(M,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
does only depend on the class
[B]
, and that the
construction is compatible with compositions.

We have morphisms

\alpha_{(M,\rho)}:r(\mathrm{tr}_{f}(M,\rho))\to(M,\rho)
and

\beta_{(N,\sigma,\nu)}:\mathrm{tr}_{f}(r(N,\sigma,\nu))\to(N,\sigma,\nu)
.
For
\alpha_{(M,\rho)}
we note that

r(\mathrm{tr}(M,\rho))=(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
such that
M^{\prime}
is a sum of the family
(N(s(y))_{y\in Y}
which can be identified with the family

(M(y))_{y\in Y}
summing to
M
. We let
\alpha_{(M,\mu)}
be the resulting isomorphism
M^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}M
.
For
\beta_{(N,\sigma,\nu)}
we note that

\mathrm{tr}_{f}(r(N,\sigma,\nu))=(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
and
N^{\prime}
is the sum of the family
(N(s(f(x)))_{x\in X}
.
For
x
in
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
we can define
g(x)
in
G
uniquely such that
g(x)s(f(x))=x
.
The family
(\rho_{g;x,g(x)s(f(x))}^{-1})_{x\in f^{-1}(Z^{c})}
induces an isomorphism from the family
(N(s(f(x)))_{x\in f^{-1}(Z^{c})}

to the family

(N(x))_{x\in f^{-1}(Z^{c})}
which sums to the summand
f^{-1}(Z^{c})N
of
N
. We let
\beta_{(N,\sigma,\nu)}
be the resulting morphism

N^{\prime}\to N
. Then
[\beta_{(N,\sigma,\nu)}]
is an isomorphism in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
.

One checks that the isomorphisms
[\alpha_{(M,\rho)}]
and
[\beta_{(N,\sigma,\nu)}]

are components of natural isomorphisms
\alpha:r\circ\mathrm{tr}_{f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathrm{id}
and

\beta:\mathrm{tr}_{f}\circ r\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathrm{id}

witnessing that

\mathrm{tr}_{f}
and
r
are inverse to each other equivalences.
∎

Recall that
{\mathbf{A}}
is an idempotent complete and sum complete compactly generated additive category. Let
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a homological functor for
k
-linear additive categories and
consider the functors

H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

In view of (
6.11
) the
Theorem

6.9
implies:

Theorem 6.10
.

The functors
H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
and
H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}
are related by a natural transfer equivalence

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}s^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}:G\mathbf{BrCov}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(6.15)

Corollary 6.11
.

The transfers or transfer equivalences are compatible with natural transformations between coarse homology theories induced by natural transformation between homological functors.

Example 6.12
.

For example, there is a natural transformation of homological functors functors

c^{GJ}:K^{\mathrm{alg}}{\mathcal{Z}}\to HC^{-}:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to D({\mathbb{Q}})

given by the
Goodwillie-Jones character.
The following square of functors on
{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}
commutes

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.0021pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.46391pt\raise .

∎

A similar statement holds for the transfer equivalences for branched coarse
G
-coverings.

7
Uncompleted topological coarse
K
-homology

In this section we introduce the equivariant uncompleted topological coarse
K
-homology
and show that it admits transfers for
G
-equivariant branched coarse coverings and transfer
equivalences for branched coarse
G
-coverings satisfying a finiteness condition
Definition

7.3
. The uncompleted topological coarse
K
-homology
is designed to be an algebraic approximation of the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology.

1
1

1

A first version of this theory was constructed by A. Engel (unpublished yet).

Analogously to the equivariant topological
K
-homology
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
constructed in
[
BE23
]
(see (
8.3
))
the uncompleted topological coarse
K
-homology depends on a (non-unital)
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
with
G
-action which admits all AV-sums and whose multiplier category
M{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete. Recall from
[
AV20
]
,
[
BEa
]
that an object decomposed into an orthogonal family
of subobjects is the AV-sum of this family if
the net of projections onto the finite partial sums converges to the identity in the strict topology of the multiplier category.

Modifying the constructions from
Section

6
we define the functor

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

which associates to a
G
-bornological space
X
the following
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive category

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.
Note that the multiplier category
M{\mathbf{C}}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive category.
For the following we interpret the term ”compact object” used in the algebraic case in
Section

6
as ”object whose identity belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}
”.
Furthermore infinite sums are interpreted as AV-sums.
The objects and morphisms
of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
are the objects
(M,\rho,\mu)
or morphisms
A:(M,\rho,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})

of
{\mathbf{V}}_{M{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
as in
Section

6
with the following additional properties:

1.

The cocycle
\rho
consist of unitary operators.

2.

The measure
\mu
takes values in selfadjoint projections.

Proposition 7.1
.

The functor
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
has the following properties:

1.

It sends coarse equivalences to equivalences.

2.

It is
u
-continuous.

3.

It sends flasque bornological coarse spaces to flasque additive categories.

4.

It sends pairs
(X,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
G\mathbf{BC}^{2}
to Karoubi filtrations
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.

5.

If
(Z,{\mathcal{Y}})
is an invariant excisive pair on
X
, then
the induced map

\frac{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Z)}{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Z\cap{\mathcal{Y}})}\to\frac{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}})}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The Properties
1
,
2
,
3
,
5

are shown by the similar arguments as for the complete version
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
in
[
BE23
, Sec. 6]
.
The Property
4
is shown by the same argument as in the algebraic case for
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}
in
[
BEKW20a
, Lem. 8.14]
using that equivariant locally finite controlled objects can be restricted to invariant subsets
Y

in the obvious manner using the assumption that
M{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete.
∎

For a functor
H:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
we consider the composition

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:=H\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathcal{C}}

(7.1)

Recall
Definition

6.2
of a homological functor.

Theorem 7.2
.

If
H
is homological, then

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}

is an equivariant coarse homology theory which is in addition strong and continuous.

Proof.

The derivation of the axioms of a coarse homology theory and of the additional properties for
H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from
Proposition

7.1
and
Definition

6.2

is essentially straightforward, see

[
BEKW20a
, Sec. 8.3]
or
[
BE23
, Sec. 7]
for similar arguments.
∎

We now construct transfers.
Let
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
be a
G
-equivariant branched coarse covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We recall
Definition

4.1
and introduce the following abbreviations.

Definition 7.3
.

1.

By
s\mathrm{fdsc}
we denote the condition that the source
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has finite dimension at coarse scales.

2.

By
\mathrm{fdsc}
we denote the condition that the target
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
eventually has finite dimension at coarse scales.

Note that
\mathrm{fdsc}
for
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
implies by
Lemma

4.4
that
also the source
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has finite dimension at coarse scales.

Theorem 7.4
.

We have a canonical natural transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fdsc}}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}},2,1}\ .

(7.2)

Proof.

We reuse the proof of
Theorem

6.6
.
On the level of objects there is no difference.
The transfer of morphisms is defined in
Theorem

6.6
using matrices.
In the present situation we must make sure that the
transferred matrices determine bounded operators.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a branched coarse covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
on
Y
.
Let
A:(M,\rho,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be a morphism
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)
which is controlled by an entourage
V
.
We let
(N,\sigma,\nu)
and
(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
denote the transferred objects in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.
Furthermore we let
(u(y):M(y)\to M)_{y\in Y}
,
(u^{\prime}(y):M^{\prime}(y)\to M^{\prime})_{y\in Y}
and

(v(x):M(f(x))\to N)_{x\in X}
,
(v^{\prime}(x):M^{\prime}(f(x))\to N^{\prime})_{x\in X}
denote the family of isometric inclusions chosen in the construction of the transfers.

We assume that
Z
is a member of
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that the parallel transport at scale
V
is defined on
Z^{c}
. We can assume that
A
is supported on
Z^{c}
. The transfer of
A
shall be determined by the matrix (
6.7
)

B_{x^{\prime},x}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}A_{f(x^{\prime}),f(x)}&(x^{\prime},x)\in P\land(f(x^{\prime}),f(x))\in V_{Z^{c}}\\
0&else\end{array}\right.\ ,

where
B_{x^{\prime},x}:M(f(x))\to M^{\prime}(f(x^{\prime}))
and
A_{y^{\prime},y}:=u^{\prime}(y^{\prime})^{*}Au(y)
for all
y,y^{\prime}
in
Y
.
For every
x
in
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
the map

B_{x}:=\sum_{x^{\prime}\in f^{-1}(Z^{c})}v^{\prime}(x^{\prime})B_{x^{\prime},x}:M(x)\to N^{\prime}

(7.3)

is well-defined since the sum has finitely many non-zero terms.
Since
A
has propagation controlled by
V
we see that if
x^{\prime}
contributes to this sum, then

f(x^{\prime})\in V[f(x)]\cap{\mathtt{supp}}(M^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
. Since

V[f(x)]
is bounded and the support is locally finite this
intersection a finite set. The contributing
x^{\prime}
are the parallel transports of
x
at scale
V

to the fibres over the points in this finite set.

We must show that

\sum_{x\in f^{-1}(Z^{c})}B_{x}v(x)^{*}

(7.4)

converges to a bounded operator

B:N\to N^{\prime}
.

We apply
Construction

4.2
which gives
a possibly larger member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
, a partition
\hat{\mathcal{W}}=(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
and a covering

{\mathcal{W}}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}
of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
such
n:=\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})<\infty
, and
U[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
and

f
is injective on
W_{i}
for every
i
in
I
, where
U:=f^{-1}(V_{Z^{c}})\cap P
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The sum in (
7.4
) converges and defines
B:N\to N^{\prime}
in
M{\mathbf{C}}
with

\|B\|\leq\sqrt{n}\|A\|\ .

Proof.

We fix
i
in
I
. We claim that

B_{i}:=\sum_{x\in\hat{W}_{i}}B_{x}v(x)^{*}:N\to N^{\prime}

is well-defined and satisfies
\|B_{i}\|\leq\|A\|
.
To this end we define the morphisms

a_{i}:=\sum_{x^{\prime}\in W_{i}}u^{\prime}(f(x^{\prime}))v(x^{\prime})^{*}:N^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}\ ,\quad b_{i}:=\sum_{x\in\hat{W}_{i}}u(f(x))v(x)^{*}:N\to M\ .

Since the subsets
W_{i}
and
\hat{W}_{i}
of
X
are bounded
and
{\mathtt{supp}}(N,\sigma,\nu)
and
{\mathtt{supp}}(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
are locally finite subsets of
X

theses sums have finitely many non-zero terms.
Since

f
is injective on
W_{i}
and
\hat{W}_{i}
the morphisms
a_{i}
and
b_{i}
are partial isometries.
More precisely,
a_{i}
is an isometry between the subobjects
\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})N
and
\mu^{\prime}(f(W_{i}))M^{\prime}
, while

b_{i}
is an isometry between
\nu(\hat{W}_{i})N
and
\mu(f(\hat{W}_{i}))M
.
We observe that

B_{i}=a_{i}^{*}Ab_{i}
since
for every
x
in
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
we have

a_{i}^{*}Ab_{i}v(x)=B_{x}
.
We can then
conclude that
\|B_{i}\|\leq\|A\|
.
This finishes the proof of the claim.

Since
(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
is a partition of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})

we have

B_{i^{\prime}}\nu(\hat{W}_{i})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}B_{i}&i=i^{\prime}\\
0&else\end{array}\right.\ .

Furthermore, by
[
BEa
, Lem. 4.15]
the object
N
is the AV-sum of the pairwise orthogonal
family of images
(\nu(\hat{W}_{i})N)_{i\in I}
.
Note that
B_{i}
is a finite partial sum of (
7.4
).
In order to show the assertion of the lemma it suffices to show (see e.g.
[
BEa
, Sec. 3]
) that

\sup_{F\subseteq I}\|\sum_{i\in F}B_{i}B_{i}^{*}\|\leq n\|A\|^{2}\ ,

where
F
runs over the finite subsets of
I
.

By construction the propagation of the matrix
(B_{x^{\prime},x})
is bounded by
U
. Thus for every
i
in
I
and
x^{\prime}
in
X
,
if
v^{\prime}(x^{\prime})^{*}B_{i}\not=0
, then
x^{\prime}\in U[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
.
This implies
B_{i}=\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})B_{i}
. We get the operator inequality

B_{i}B_{i}^{*}=\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})B_{i}B_{i}^{*}\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})\leq\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|B_{i}B_{i}^{*}\|\leq\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|A\|^{2}\ .

This gives

\|\sum_{i\in F}B_{i}B_{i}^{*}\|\leq\|A\|^{2}\|\sum_{i\in F}\nu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|\leq n\|A\|^{2}

since the family
{\mathcal{W}}

has multiplicity bounded by
n
.
∎

Lemma

7.5
finishes the proof of
Theorem

7.4
.
∎

Recall that
{\mathbf{C}}
is a
C^{*}
-category with
G
-action
which admits all AV-sums and such that
M{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete.
Let
H:\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
be a homological functor for
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear additive categories
Definition

6.2
.
Recall
Theorem

7.2
.

Corollary 7.6
.

The equivariant coarse homology functor

H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
admits the structure of transfers

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to s^{*}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fdsc}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(7.5)

We now assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
has the trivial
G
-action. We consider the functors

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:\mathbf{BC}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ .

Theorem 7.7
.

The transfer from (
7.2
) restricts to a
natural transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fdsc}}\to\mathbf{Add}_{{\mathbb{C}},2,1}\ .

(7.6)

which becomes an equivalence after further restriction
G\mathbf{BrCov}^{\mathrm{fdsc}}
.

Proof.

We reuse that argument for
Theorem

6.9
.
The construction of the natural transformation in (
7.6
)
by restriction of the one from
Theorem

7.4
is the same as in the proof of
Theorem

6.9
.
In order to show that it is an equivalence, we again construct for every branched coarse
G
-covering

(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
an inverse

r:{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\to{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

(7.7)

The construction of
r
on the level of objects is the same as in
Theorem

6.9
.
The value
A:(M,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
of
r
on a morphism
B:(N,\sigma,\nu)\to(N^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
was defined in terms of a matrix
(A_{y^{\prime},y})
in (
6.14
).
In the present case we must again must make sure that this matrix determines a bounded operator.

Let
U
denote the propagation of
B
and set
V:=f(U)
.
For every
y
in
Y
we define

A_{y}:=\sum_{y^{\prime}\in Y}u^{\prime}(y^{\prime})A_{y^{\prime},y}:N(s(y))\to M\ .

This sum has finitely many non-zero summands since the matrix
(A_{y^{\prime},y})
has propagation
V:=f(U)

and there are only finitely may
y^{\prime}
in
V[y]
with
0\not=A_{y^{\prime},y}
.
We must show that

\sum_{y\in Y}A_{y}u(y)^{*}

(7.8)

converges and defines a bounded morphism
A:M\to M^{\prime}

The construction
Construction

4.3
provides a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
, a covering
{\mathcal{W}}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}
with
\tilde{V}:=\mathrm{bd}({\mathcal{W}})\in{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}
and
n:=\mathrm{mult}({\mathcal{W}})<\infty
, and
a partition
\hat{\mathcal{W}}=(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
of
Z^{c}
such that
V(W_{i})\subseteq\hat{W}_{i}
for all
i
in
I
and the parallel transport at scale
\tilde{V}
is defined on
Z^{c}
.

Lemma 7.8
.

The sum in (
7.8
) converges and defines
A:M\to M^{\prime}
in
M{\mathbf{C}}
with

\|A\|\leq\sqrt{n}\|B\|\ .

Proof.

We fix
i
in
I
and
define

A_{i}:=\sum_{y\in\hat{W}_{i}}A_{y}u(y)^{*}\ .

Since
\hat{W}_{i}
is
\tilde{V}
-bounded the sum has finitely many non-zero terms.
We claim that
\|A_{i}\|\leq\|B\|
.
We fix a base point
y_{i}
in
\hat{W}_{i}
and choose a section
t:W_{i}\to f^{-1}(W_{i})
uniquely determined by the condition
that
t(y^{\prime})
in
f^{-1}(y^{\prime})
is the parallel transport of
s(y_{i})
. This is possible since

\hat{W}_{i}
is
\tilde{V}
-bounded and the parallel transport at scale
\tilde{V}
is defined on
Z^{c}
.
We define the function
h:W_{i}\to G
such that
h(y^{\prime})t(y^{\prime})=s(y^{\prime})
.
We further define

a_{i}:=\sum_{y\in\hat{W}_{i}}\sigma^{-1}_{h(y)}v(s(y))u(y)^{*}:M\to N\ ,\quad b_{i}:=\sum_{y^{\prime}\in W_{i}}u^{\prime}(y^{\prime})v^{\prime}(s(y^{\prime}))^{*}\sigma^{\prime}_{h(y^{\prime})}:N^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}\ .

Since
W_{i}
and
\hat{W}_{i}
are bounded these sums have finitely many non-zero terms.
Since
s
is injective these maps are partial isometries. More precisely,

a_{i}
is an isometry between
\mu(\hat{W}_{i})
and
\nu(t(\hat{W}_{i}))N
, and

b_{i}
is an isometry from
\nu^{\prime}(t(W_{i}))N^{\prime}
to
\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})M^{\prime}
.
We observe that

A_{i}=b_{i}Ba_{i}
since for every
y
in
Z^{c}
one can check that

A_{y}=b_{i}Ba_{i}u(y)
.
We further conclude that
\|A_{i}\|\leq\|B\|
.

Since
(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
is a partition of
Z^{c}

we have

A_{i^{\prime}}\mu(\hat{W}_{i})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}A_{i}&i=i^{\prime}\\
0&else\end{array}\right.\ .

Furthermore, by
[
BEa
, Lem. 4.15]
the object
M
is the AV-sum of the pairwise orthogonal
family of images
(\mu(\hat{W}_{i})M)_{i\in I}
.
Note that
A_{i}
is a finite partial sum of (
7.8
).
In order to show the assertion of the lemma it suffices to show that

\sup_{F\subseteq I}\|\sum_{i\in F}A_{i}A_{i}^{*}\|\leq n\|B\|^{2}\ ,

where
F
runs over the finite subsets of
I
.

Since the matrix
(A_{y^{\prime},y})
is
V
-controlled and
V[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
we have the equality

A_{i}=\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})A_{i}
for every
i
in
I
.
We furthermore have the operator inequality

A_{i}A_{i}^{*}=\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})A_{i}A_{i}^{*}\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})\leq\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|A_{i}\|^{2}\ .

Using
n=\mathrm{mult}(\hat{\mathcal{W}})
we get for every finite subset
F
of
I

\|\sum_{i\in F}A_{i}A_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\|B\|^{2}\|\sum_{i\in F}\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|\leq n\|B\|^{2}

which implies the desired estimate.
∎

Lemma

7.8
finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

8
The transfer for topological coarse
K
-homology

In this section we show that the equivariant topological coarse
K
-homology
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}

constructed in
[
BE23
]
has transfers or transfer equivalences for branched coarse coverings under finite asymptotic dimension assumptions.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a
C^{*}
-category with
G
-action which admits all AV-sums and whose multiplier category
M{\mathbf{C}}
is idempotent complete. We then have the functor

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}

which associates to every
G
-bornological coarse space the
C^{*}
-category

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
of equivariant locally locally finite objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
introduced in
[
BE23
, Sec. 5]

2
2

2

In the notation of the reference
[
BE23
]
the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
from the present paper denoted by
\overline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{\mathrm{lf}}
.
.

Note that the involution on
M{\mathbf{C}}
induces an involution on
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
described in
Section

7
so that
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
becomes a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-category. It further inherts a
C^{*}
-norm from

.
By an inspection of the definitions in
Section

7
and
[
BE23
, Sec. 5]
we have an equivalence

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:=\overline{{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\ ,

(8.1)

i.e.
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
is the completion of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
in the

-norm induced from
M{\mathbf{C}}
.

Let
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
be a branched coarse covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Definition 8.1
.

1.

By
s\mathrm{fadim}
we denote the condition that the source
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has finite asymptotic dimension.

2.

By
\mathrm{fadim}
we denote the condition that the target

(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
eventually has finite asymptotic dimension.

Note that eventually finite asymptotic dimension of he target
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
implies by
Lemma

4.4
that
also the source
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has finite asymptotic dimension.

We let
\ell:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
denote the Dwyer-Kan localization at the unitary equivalences.
We realize
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
by the strict
2
-category of unital
C^{*}
-categories, unital functors and unitary equivalences.

Theorem 8.2
.

We have a canonical natural transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fadim}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\ .

(8.2)

Proof.

We reuse the proof of
Theorem

7.4
.
Instead of a quotient by a Karoubi filtration we now have

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})=\frac{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)}

where
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
is the ideal in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
obtained by completing the Karoubi filtration
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}})
. Note that
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\cap{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)={\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
We can therefore define the transfer
on the dense wide subcategory
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}})

and then extend it by continuity.

Assume that
(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
eventually has asymptotic dimension
n-1
.
We take this integer to bound the multiplicities of the coverings
{\mathcal{W}}
used in the proof of
Lemma

7.5
.
By
Lemma

7.5
the norm of the transfer of a controlled morphism
A
is then bounded by
\sqrt{n}\|A\|
.
Thus the norm of the uncompleted transfer map
\mathrm{tr}_{f}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
is bounded by
\sqrt{n}
. It therefore extends by continuity to the desired transfer
\mathrm{tr}_{f}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
between the completed categories.

We finally observe that the morphism
t_{\Phi}
in (
6.9
) is unitary by construction.
So the fillers of the squares are implemented by unitary natural isomorphisms.
∎

We consider the composition

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:=K^{\mathrm{top}}\circ{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(8.3)

The following is taken from

]
.

.

The functor

86.8759pt\raise-8.81375pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0605pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{K^{\mathrm{top}}_{2,1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.20308pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 138.11714pt\raise-31.69293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We conclude an equivalence of functors

K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq K^{\mathrm{top}}_{2,1}\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(8.4)

Therefore
Theorem

8.2
immediately implies:

Theorem 8.4
.

The functor
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
admits the structure of transfers

tr:t^{*}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to s^{*}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:{}^{G}\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fadim}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(8.5)

We now assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
has the trivial
G
-action. We consider the functors

{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:\mathbf{BC}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ .

Theorem 8.5
.

The transfer from (
8.2
) restricts to a transfer

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}s^{*}\ell{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fadim}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\ .

(8.6)

which becomes an equivalence after further restriction to
G\mathbf{BrCov}^{\mathrm{fadim}}
.

Proof.

We reuse the proof of
Theorem

7.7
.
Let
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
be in
G\mathbf{BrCov}^{\mathrm{fadim}}
.
Assume that
(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
eventually has asymptotic dimension
n-1
. Then we can choose the coverings
in the construction of the inverse
r
in (
7.7
) on the dense subcategory

{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
of
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
all with multiplicity
n
.
By
Lemma

7.8
the functor
r
in (
7.7
) is bounded by
\sqrt{n}
and therefore extends by continuity to

r:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\to{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
.

The transformations
\alpha
and
\beta
constructed at the end of the proof of
Theorem

6.9
are implemented by unitaries and therefore again witness that
r
is an inverse of the transfer in (
8.6
).
∎

Remark 8.6
.

In the special case of a asymptotically faithful covering of a space of graphs an analogue of

Theorem

8.5
was shown in
[
WY12
, Lem. 3.12]
.
Instead of the finite asymptotic dimension assumption on the source in this reference one assumes the uniform operator norm
localization property
[
WY12
, Def. 3.10]

∎

Remark 8.7
.

In the proofs of
Theorem

8.2
and
Theorem

8.5
we have shown that the transfer functors
and its inverse were bounded by
\sqrt{n}
, where
n-1
was the relevant asymptotic dimension.
As functors between
C^{*}
-categories are automatically contractions we a posteriori can conclude that the norms of these functors are bounded by
1
.
∎

Corollary 8.8
.

The functors
K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
are related by a natural transfer transformation

\mathrm{tr}:t^{*}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to s^{*}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}:G\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fadim}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(8.7)

which becomes an
equivalence after further restriction to
G\mathbf{BrCov}^{\mathrm{fadim}}
.

Remark 8.9
.

It follows from the example discussed in
Section

12
that the
natural transfer transformation (
8.7
)
is not an equivalence in general if we drop the finite asymptotic dimension assumption on the domain.
∎

Let
Y
be a bornological coarse space with a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
(M,\mu)
,
(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
be
Y
-controlled
objects in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)
.
Following G. Yu we adopt the following notion.

Definition 8.10
.

A morphism
A:(M,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)
is called a ghost if
for every coarse entourage
U
of
Y
we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\sup_{x,x^{\prime}\in Z^{c}}\|\mu^{\prime}(U[x^{\prime}])A\mu(U[x])\|=0\ .

Lemma 8.11
.

If
Y
has finite asymptotic dimension, then
a ghost represents zero in the quotient
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

\limsup_{Z\in{\mathcal{Y}}}\|\mu^{\prime}(Z^{c})A\mu(Z^{c})\|=0\ .

(8.8)

We will use similar estimates as in the proof of
Lemma

7.5
.
Let
n-1
bound the asymptotic dimension of
Y
.
We fix
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
and choose
a controlled
A^{\prime}:(M,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
such that
\|A-A^{\prime}\|\leq\epsilon
.
Let
V
be a bound of the propagation of
A^{\prime}
.
We can choose a coarsely bounded partition
\hat{\mathcal{W}}=(\hat{W}_{i})_{i\in I}
of
Y

and a coarsely bounded covering
{\mathcal{W}}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}
of multiplicity bounded by
n
such that

V[\hat{W}_{i}]\subseteq W_{i}
for all
i
in
I
. Then the sum

A^{\prime}=\sum_{i\in I}A^{\prime}_{i}
converges strongly, where
A^{\prime}_{i}:=\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})A^{\prime}\mu(\hat{W}_{i})
.

Let
I_{Z}:=\{i\in I\mid W_{i}\cap Z^{c}\not=\emptyset\}
.
For a finite subset
F
of
I_{Z}
we have

\sum_{i,j\in F}A^{\prime}_{i}A^{\prime,*}_{j}=\sum_{i\in F}A^{\prime}_{i}A_{i}^{\prime,*}\ .

Therefore

\|\sum_{i,j\in F}A^{\prime}_{i}A^{\prime,*}_{j}\|\leq\sup_{i\in I_{Z}}\|A^{\prime}_{i}\|^{2}\|\sum_{i\in F}\mu^{\prime}(W_{i})\|\leq n\sup_{i\in I_{Z}}\|A^{\prime}_{i}\|^{2}

and hence

\|\sum_{i\in I_{Z}}A_{i}\|^{2}=\sup_{F\subseteq I_{Z}}\||\sum_{i,j\in F}A^{\prime}_{i}A^{\prime,*}_{j}\|\leq n\sup_{i\in I_{Z}}\|A^{\prime}_{i}\|^{2}\ .

Since
A
was a ghost we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\sup_{i\in I_{Z}}\|A^{\prime}_{i}\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.
Hence

\limsup_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\|\sum_{i\in I_{Z}}A^{\prime}_{i}\|\leq\sqrt{n}\epsilon
.
This implies

\limsup_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\|\mu^{\prime}(Z^{c})A\mu(Z^{c})\|\leq\epsilon+\sqrt{n}\epsilon
.
Since
\epsilon
was arbitrary we conclude (
8.8
).
∎

Corollary 8.12
.

If
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
is a branched coarse covering such that
X
has finite asymptotic dimension, then
the transfer
\mathrm{tr}:\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to\ell{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}{\mathcal{Z}})
annihilates ghost operators.

Proof.

The transfer preserves ghosts by construction. But ghosts represent zero in

{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X,f^{-1}{\mathcal{Z}})
by
Lemma

8.11
.

∎

9
Traces on
C^{*}
-categories of controlled objects

In this section we first recall the concept of a trace on a
C^{*}
-category and show that an everywhere defined trace
induces a trace function on
K
-theory. The main result of this section is the construction of traces on the category of equivariant
{\mathbf{C}}
-controlled objects
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
from a trace on the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}
.

We consider a possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Definition 9.1
.

1.

A
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued trace on
{\mathbf{C}}
consists of a subset
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
of objects of
{\mathbf{C}}
which is closed under isomorphism in
M{\mathbf{C}}
and a collection of linear maps

\tau:=(\tau_{C})_{C\in{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)}\ ,\qquad\tau_{C}:{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)\to{\mathbb{C}}

such that for every two morphisms

f:C\to C^{\prime}
and
g:C^{\prime}\to C
with
C,C^{\prime}\in{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
we have
\tau_{C^{\prime}}(f\circ g)=\tau_{C}(g\circ f)
.

2.

We say that
\tau
on
{\mathbf{C}}
is continuous if the maps
\tau_{C}
are continuous for all objects
C
in
{\mathbf{C}}
.

3.

We say that
\tau
is positive if
\tau_{C}(f)\geq 0
whenever
f\geq 0
.

We will usually omit the subscripts
C
and denote the members
\tau_{C}
of
\tau
simply also by
\tau
.

Example 9.2
.

Recall the inclusion functor
\mathrm{incl}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
If
\tau
is a trace on a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
, then it is a trace on the one-object
C^{*}
-category
\mathrm{incl}(A)
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Assume that
A
is unital. Then the trace canonically extends to a trace on
the category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(A)
of Hilbert
A
-modules and compact morphisms with domain
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A)
.

If
\tau
is continuous or positive, then so is its extension.

∎

Example 9.3
.

The identity is a trace
\mathrm{tr}:{\mathbb{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
. It extends to the usual matrix trace
\mathrm{tr}
on
the
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
of Hilbert spaces and compact operators with domain
\dim(\mathrm{tr})=\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
the finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces. Note that
\mathrm{tr}
is continuous and positive.

∎

Example 9.4
.

If
G
is a group and
[g]
is a finite conjugacy class in
G
, then we have a continuous trace

\tau_{[g]}:C_{r}^{*}(G)\to{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\qquad f\mapsto\sum_{g^{\prime}\in[g]}f(g^{\prime})

on the reduced group
C^{*}
-algebra,
where we consider
C^{*}_{r}(G)
as a linear subspace of the complex-valued functions on
G
.
We therefore get a continuous extension of
\tau_{[g]}
to the
C^{*}
-category category
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}(C_{r}^{*}(G))
with domain
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau_{[g]})=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(C_{r}^{*}(G))
. The trace
\tau_{[e]}
is also positive.

∎

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a
C^{*}
-category with a trace
\tau
.
Recall that
K^{\mathrm{top}}
denotes the topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories,
Definition

13.17
.

Proposition 9.5
.

If

{\mathbf{C}}
is unital and
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)={\mathbf{C}}
, then
\tau
induces a
homomorphism

\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}:K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbb{C}}\ .

(9.1)

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{\oplus}
be an additive completion.
By Morita invariance of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
(see
Remark

13.18
) it induces an equivalence

K^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{C}}_{\oplus})
.
Furthermore, the trace
\tau
canonically extends from
{\mathbf{C}}
to
{\mathbf{C}}_{\oplus}
.

We can therefore in addition assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is additive.
In this case we can use the description of
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}({\mathbf{C}})
given in
[
BEa
, Lem. 15.3]
.
Every class
p
in
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}({\mathbf{C}})
can be represented by
a pair of projections
(P,\tilde{P})
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
for some object
C
of
{\mathbf{C}}
.
Furthermore,
p=0
if and only if
exists a partial isometry

U
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
such that
UU^{*}=P
and
U^{*}U=\tilde{P}
.
A second pair
(P^{\prime},\tilde{P}^{\prime})
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C^{\prime})
represents the same
class
p
if and only if the class represented by
(P\oplus\tilde{P}^{\prime},\tilde{P}\oplus P^{\prime})
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C\oplus C^{\prime})
represents
0
.
Finally, the sum
p+p^{\prime}
in
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}({\mathbf{C}})
is represented by

(P\oplus P^{\prime},\tilde{P}\oplus\tilde{P}^{\prime})
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C\oplus C^{\prime})
.

We define the homomorphism

\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}:K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\qquad\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}(p):=\tau(P)-\tau(\tilde{P})\ .

Using the facts above it is straightforward to check that

\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}
is well-defined and a homomorphism.
∎

Example 9.6
.

The
homomorphism

\mathrm{tr}^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}:K^{C^{*}}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}}))\to{\mathbb{C}}

is the dimension homomorphism and takes values in the integers.
∎

Example 9.7
.

Let
A
be a unital
C^{*}
-algebra with a trace
\tau

and consider its extension to
\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A)
.
The inclusion
\mathrm{incl}(A)\to\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A)
of unital
C^{*}
-categories is a Morita equivalence. Let
K^{C^{*}}
denote the topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras. Then

K^{C^{*}}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwerfwerfw}}}{{\simeq}}K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{incl}(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A))\ .

(9.2)

The induced homomorphism

K^{C^{*}}_{0}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwergkop0werf}}}{{\cong}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{0}(\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{C}}

coincides with the homomorphism on
C^{*}
-algebra
K
-theory
classically associated to
\tau
.

∎

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a
C^{*}
-category with a
G
-action and a trace
\tau
.

Definition 9.8
.

We say that
\tau
is
G
-invariant if
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
is
G
-invariant and
\tau_{gC}(gA)=\tau_{C}(A)
for every morphism
A
in
{\mathbf{C}}
and
g
in
G
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a
C^{*}
-category with a
G
-action and an invariant trace
\tau
.
We assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
admits all
AV
-sums and
is idempotent complete. We further assume that the unital objects belong to the domain of
\tau
, i.e., that
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
.

For a
G
-bornological coarse space
X
we consider the
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
of equivariant
X
-controlled objects in
{\mathbf{C}}
described by (
8.1
).

We consider and object
(H,\rho,\mu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.
For a point
x
in
X
we choose an isometry
u(x):C\to H
in
M{\mathbf{C}}
representing the image of
\mu(\{x\})
.
For
A
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}((H,\rho,\mu))
we then set
A_{x,x}:=u(x)^{*}Au(x)
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C)
.

Lemma 9.9
.

The map

\tau_{x}:{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}((H,\rho,\mu))\to{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\quad A\mapsto\tau(A_{x,x})\ .

(9.3)

is well-defined independent of the choices and satisfies

\tau^{G}_{gx}=\tau^{G}_{x}

(9.4)

for every
g
in
G
.

Proof.

By local finiteness of
(H,\rho,\mu)
the object
C
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
.
Therefore the complex number
\tau(A_{x,x})
is defined.

If
u^{\prime}(x):C^{\prime}\to H
is another representative of the image of
\mu(\{x\})
and
A_{x,x}^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(x)^{*}Au^{\prime}(x)
, then
w:=u(x)^{*}u^{\prime}(x):C^{\prime}\to C
is a unitary isomorphism and

A_{x,x}=wA_{x,x}^{\prime}w^{*}
. Using the trace property of
\tau
this implies that
\tau(A_{x,x})=\tau(A_{x,x}^{\prime})
.
Consequently,
\tau_{x}(A)
is well-defined.

We claim that

\tau^{G}_{gx}(A)=\tau^{G}_{x}(A)

(9.5)

for every
g
in
G
.
By
G
-invariance of
A
and
\mu
we have

\rho_{g}A\rho_{g}^{-1}=gA

and
\rho_{g}^{-1}\ g\mu(\{x\})\ \rho_{g}=\mu(\{gx\})
.
If
u(x):C\to H
represents the image of
\mu(\{x\})
,
then
\rho_{g}^{-1}\ gu(x)\ :gC\to H
represents the image of
\mu(\{gx\})
.
With this choice

A_{gx,gx}=gu(x)^{*}\ \rho_{g}A\rho_{g}^{-1}\ gu(x)=g(A_{x,x})\ .

(9.6)

Using the
G
-invariance of
\tau
we conclude (
9.5
).
∎

A
G
-invariant subset
Y
of
X
is called
G
-bounded if there exists a bounded subset
B
of
Y
such that
GB=Y
.

Definition 9.10
.

By
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)
we denote the
G
-set of coarse components of
X
.

We consider a
G
-orbit
i
in
\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(X)/G
and let
X_{i}
be the corresponding
G
-invariant subspace of
X
.

Consider an object
(H,\rho,\mu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)
. We assume that
{\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)\cap X_{i}
is
G
-bounded.
We can choose a bounded set-theoretic fundamental domain

F
for the
G
-action on
{\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)\cap X_{i}
. For every
x
in
X
we let
G_{x}
denote the stabilizer
G_{x}
of
x
in
G
.
For
A
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}((H,\rho,\mu))
we define the complex number

\tau^{G}_{i,(H,\rho,\mu)}(A):=\sum_{x\in F}\frac{1}{|G_{x}|}\tau_{x}(A)\ ,

(9.7)

where we interpret
1/\infty
as
0
.

Lemma 9.11
.

If
G
acts with finite stabilizers and
\tau
is continuous, then

\tau^{G}_{X,(H,\rho,\mu)}
is well-defined, independent of the choice of
F
. The family
of maps
(\tau^{G}_{i,(H,\rho,\mu)})_{(H,\rho,\mu)}
is a continuous trace
\tau^{G}_{i}
on
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
with

{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau^{G}_{i})
consisting of all objects with the property that
{\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)\cap X_{i}
is
G
-bounded.

Proof.

By assumption the set
{\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)

is a
G
-invariant locally finite subset. In particular,

{\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)\cap F
is a finite set.
This shows that the sum in (
9.7
) is finite and that the formula defines a continuous linear map

\tau^{G}_{i,(H,\rho,\mu)}:{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)}((H,\rho,\mu))\to{\mathbb{C}}\ .

Since
|G_{x}|=|G_{gx}|
for every
x
in
X
and
g
in
G

it follows from (
9.4
) that
\tau_{i,(H,\rho,\mu)}^{G}(A)
does not depend on the choice of the fundamental domain
F
.

It remains to check the trace property. From now on in order to simplify the notation we drop the index indicating the controlled Hilbert space. We consider
A:(H,\rho,\mu)\to(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
and
B:(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})\to(H,\rho,\mu)
. We fix a bounded fundamental domain
F
for
({\mathtt{supp}}(H,\rho,\mu)\cup(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))\cap X_{i}
. We first assume that
A
and
B
are

U
-controlled for some entourage
U
of
X
.
Then for every
x
in
X_{i}
the sets
\{y\in X\mid A_{y,x}\not=0\}
and
\{y\in X\mid B_{x,y}\not=0\}
are finite subsets of
X_{i}
. The first is contained in
{\mathtt{supp}}(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})\cap U[\{x\}]
which is finite since
{\mathtt{supp}}(H^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is locally finite and
U[\{x\}]
is bounded.
For the second we argue similarly.
We calculate

\displaystyle\tau^{G}_{i}(BA)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in F,y\in X}\frac{1}{|G_{x}|}\tau(B_{x,y}A_{y,x})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in F,y\in F,g\in G}\frac{1}{|G_{x}||G_{y}|}\tau(B_{x,gy}A_{gy,x})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in F,y\in F,g\in G}\frac{1}{|G_{g^{-1}x}||G_{y}|}\tau(B_{g^{-1}x,y}A_{y,g^{-1}x})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in F,y\in F,g\in G}\frac{1}{|G_{g^{-1}x}||G_{y}|}\tau(A_{y,g^{-1}x}B_{g^{-1}x,y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in X,y\in F}\frac{1}{|G_{y}|}\tau(A_{y,x}B_{x,y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{G}(AB)

All sums have finitely many non-zero terms.
For the step marked by
!
it is important that the groups
G_{y}
are finite and that only points
y
in
X_{i}
contribute.
For
!!
we use the
G
-equivariance of the trace and relations like (
9.6
), and for
!!!
the trace property. General
A,B
can be approximated in norm by controlled morphisms. We conclude the trace property by continuous extension using the continuity of
\tau
.
∎

Recall that
i
is in
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G
and
X_{i}
is the corresponding
G
-invariant subset of
X
.
If
X_{i}
is
G
-bounded, then
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau^{G}_{i})={\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
.
Note that
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
is unital and
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\cong K_{0}^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X))
.

Definition 9.12
.

If
X_{i}
is
G
-bounded and
G
acts with finite stabilizers on
X_{i}
, then we define

\tau^{K,G}_{i}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to{\mathbb{C}}

as the homomorphism associated by
Proposition

9.5
to the trace
\tau^{G}_{i}
.

If
G
is trivial, then we omit it as a superscript. Recall that we also omit the subscript
{\mathbf{C}}
in case that
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
with the trivial
G
-action.
In this case we consider the canonical trace from
Example

9.3
.
Finally, if
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G
is a singleton, then we also omit the subscript
i
.

Example 9.13
.

If
G
is trivial and
X
is bounded and coarsely connected, then

{\mathbf{V}}(X)\simeq\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
and
\tau^{K}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(X)\to{\mathbb{C}}
takes values in
{\mathbb{Z}}
.
∎

Example 9.14
.

We assume that
G
is non-trivial and acts freely on
X
, and that
X

G
-bounded and coarsely connected. For any
x
in
X
the map
G\to X
,
g\mapsto gx
induces a coarse equivalence

G_{can,min}\to X
. Under the isomorphism

\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\cong\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(G_{can,min})\cong K^{C^{*}}_{0}(C_{r}^{*}(G))

the trace homomorphism
\tau^{K,G}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)\to{\mathbb{C}}
corresponds to
the homomorphism
\tau_{[e]}^{K^{C^{*}}}:\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(C^{*}_{r}(G))\to{\mathbb{C}}
induced by the trace
\tau_{[e]}:C^{*}_{r}(G)\to{\mathbb{C}}
in
(
9.4
).

∎

Example 9.15
.

If
M
is a compact Riemannian manifold with a Dirac operator
\not{D}
of degree
0
.
Then
M
is a bounded bornological coarse space with the metric structures.
We have the coarse index class

\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D})\in\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(X)
. In this case

\tau^{K}(\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D}))
is the Fredholm index of
\not{D}
.
∎

Example 9.16
.

If
M
is a
G
-compact Riemannian
G
-manifold with an invariant generalized Dirac operator
\not{D}
of degree
0
, then
we have the equivariant coarse index index class
\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(\not{D})\in\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X)
.
Then
\tau^{K,G}(\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(\not{D}))
is the classical
L^{2}
-index of
\not{D}
introduced in
[
Ati76
]
.
∎

10
The algebraic
L^{2}
-index theorem

Let
k
be a commutative base ring and
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
denote the category of
k
-linear categories.
We consider a
k
-linear category
{\mathbf{A}}
. The following is the algebraic analogue of
Definition

9.1
.

Definition 10.1
.

A trace on
{\mathbf{A}}
consists of a subset
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
of objects of
{\mathbf{A}}
which is closed under isomorphisms and a collection of
k
-linear maps

\tau:=(\tau_{C})_{C\in{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)}\ ,\qquad\tau_{C}:{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(C)\to k

such that for every two morphisms

f:C\to C^{\prime}
and
g:C^{\prime}\to C
with
C,C^{\prime}\in{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
we have
\tau_{C^{\prime}}(f\circ g)=\tau_{C}(g\circ f)
.

We let
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr}}
denote the category of pairs

({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
of a
k
-linear category
{\mathbf{A}}
and a trace

\tau
. A morphism
({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})\to({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
is a
k
-linear functor

\phi:{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{A}}
such that
\phi({\mathtt{dom}}(\tau^{\prime}))\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
and
\tau_{\phi(C^{\prime})}(\phi(A^{\prime}))=\tau^{\prime}_{C^{\prime}}(A^{\prime})
for all objects
C^{\prime}
in
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau^{\prime})
and endomorphisms
A^{\prime}
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}(C^{\prime})
.

We have a forgetful functor
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathrm{trc}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}
which forgets the traces.
We let furthermore
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
denote the full category
of pairs
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
with
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)={\mathbf{A}}
. We consider a functor
H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}
.

Definition 10.2
.

A tracing of
H
is a natural transformation

\mathrm{trc}^{H}:\pi_{0}H\circ{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathrm{trc}}\to{\mathtt{const}}_{k}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}\to{\mathbf{Ab}}\ .

(10.1)

We let
\tau^{H}:\pi_{0}H({\mathbf{A}})\to k
denote the evaluation of the tracing
\mathrm{trc}^{H}
at
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
.

Remark 10.3
.

With the obvious modifications of definitions we can consider

({\mathbf{C}},\tau)\mapsto(\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}:\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbb{C}})

from (
9.1
)
as a the natural transformation of functors from
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}

and therefore as a tracing
\mathrm{trc}^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}
of the functor
K^{\mathrm{top}}
.
∎

Example 10.4
.

Recall that
{\mathcal{Z}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is the forgetful functor.
The algebraic
K
-theory functor
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{Z}}
for
k
-linear categories is traced.
The argument is analogous to the one from
Proposition

9.5
.
We just replace
the word unitary by invertible and
U^{*}
by
U^{-1}
.
The naturality is obvious from the construction.

If
{\mathbb{Q}}\subseteq k
, then alternatively one can construct the tracing via cyclic homology. More precisely,
the tracing of homotopy
K
-theory from
Lemma

13.30
induces the tracing of algebraic
K
-theory
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{Z}}
by restriction along the comparison map (
13.41
).

∎

Example 10.5
.

If
R
is a unital
k
-algebra with a trace
\kappa
, then
the endofunctor
R\otimes_{k}-:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}

lifts to an endofunctor
R\otimes_{k}-:{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{k}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{k}
.
To this end we note that a trace
\tau
on
{\mathbf{A}}
induces
a trace
\kappa\otimes\tau
on
R\otimes_{k}{\mathbf{A}}
uniquely characterized by

(\kappa\otimes\tau)(r\otimes A)=\kappa(r)\tau(A)
.

Let now
R
be a possibly non-unital
k
-algebra with a
trace
\kappa:R\to k
.
If
H
is a traced homological functor (we only need split-exact) for
k
-linear categories, then
the twist
H_{R}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is again traced. In order to construct this tracing we first
extend the trace to a trace
\tau^{+}
on the unitalization

R^{+}\cong R\oplus k
such that
\kappa^{+}_{|k}=0
and
\kappa^{+}_{|R}=\kappa
.
Then
H_{R^{+}}
is traced.
We then define
\tau^{H_{R}}:\pi_{0}H_{R}({\mathbf{A}})\to k
as the restriction of

\tau^{H_{R^{+}}}:\pi_{0}H_{R^{+}}({\mathbf{A}})\to k

along the inclusion
\pi_{0}H_{R}({\mathbf{A}})\to\pi_{0}H_{R^{+}}({\mathbf{A}})
. See also
Example

6.3
.
5
.

∎

If
{\mathbf{A}}
is a
k
-linear category with a
G
-action, then

G
-invariance of a trace on
{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as in
Definition

9.8
.

We let
G\mathbf{Add}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr},\sharp}
denote the category of
idempotent and sum complete compactly generated additive categories with
G
-action and with an invariant trace
such that
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
contains all compact objects,
and equivariant compact-object and trace preserving
k
-linear functors.
Let
X
be a
G
-bornological coarse space.

Lemma 10.6
.

If
G
acts with finite stabilizers on
X
, then for every
i
in
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G

the functor
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)
from
Lemma

6.1
lifts to a functor

({\mathbf{A}},\tau)\to({\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X),\tau^{G}_{i}):G\mathbf{Add}_{k}^{\mathrm{tr},\sharp}\to\mathbf{Add}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{k}\ ,

where
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau^{G}_{i})
consists of all objects
(M,\rho,\mu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)
such that

{\mathtt{supp}}(M,\rho,\mu)\cap X_{i}
is
G
-bounded.

Proof.

We repeat the construction from
Lemma

9.11
.
We can drop the steps discussing completions.
The naturality of the construction in
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
is obvious.
∎

If
G
acts with finite stabilizers on
X
, then we have a vector-valued trace

\tau^{G}_{X}:=(\tau^{G}_{i})_{i\in\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(X)/G}:={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)\to\prod_{\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(X)/G}k

(10.2)

defined on objects with coarse component-wise bounded support.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a branched coarse
G
-covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We assume that the coarse components of
Y
are bounded and that the coarse
G
-components of
X
are
G
-bounded. Note that
G
acts freely on
X
by assumption.

We assume that every member of
{\mathcal{Z}}
is a union of coarse components.
We then introduce the following abbreviations:

T(X):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\frac{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}k}{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(f^{-1}(Z))/G}k}\ ,\quad T(Y):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\frac{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}k}{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Z)}k}\ .

We have a pull-back map

f^{*}:T(Y)\to T(X)\ .

Lemma 10.7
.

We get induced every-where defined vector-valued traces

\bar{\tau}^{G}_{X}:=([(\tau^{G}_{j})_{j\in\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}]:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\to T(X)\ ,\quad\bar{\tau}_{Y}:=[(\tau_{i})_{i\in\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}]:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to T(Y)\ .

Proof.

We consider the second map. The argument for the first is similar.
Note that morphisms
[A]
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
are represented by morphisms

A:(M,\mu)\to(M^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)
, and
[A]=0
if and only if
A
factorizes
over an object coming from
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}({\mathcal{Z}})
. Let
Z
be a member of
{\mathcal{Z}}
containing the support of this object. Then indeed
\tau_{i}(A)=0
for all
i
in
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)
with
Y_{i}\subseteq Z^{c}
.
∎

Remark 10.8
.

Let
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
be a branched coarse covering relative to the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
. We assume that the members of
{\mathcal{Z}}
are unions of coarse components.
Let
C
be a coarse component of
X
. Then
f(C)
is contained in a coarse component
D
of
Y
, but in general is not equal to
D
.

Assume that there exists an entourage
V
of
Y
such that
D
is already coarsely connected in
Y_{V}
and
that the parallel transport at scale
V
is defined on
D
.
Then any two points in
D
are connected by a
V
-path. Using the path-lifting
we can conclude that
f(C)\to D
is surjective.

Consequently, if
Y
has a generating entourage
V
, then we can choose
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that for every coarse component

C
in
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
the image
f(C)
is a coarse component of
Y
.
The latter property is an assumption in
Proposition

10.9
below.
∎

Recall that we assume that the coarse components of
Y
are bounded and that the coarse
G
-components of
X
are
G
-bounded.
By
Theorem

6.9
the transfer induces an equivalence

\mathrm{tr}_{f}:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ .

Proposition 10.9
.

We assume that the members of
{\mathcal{Z}}
are unions of coarse components and that there exists
Z
in
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
f
sends
every coarse component of
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
surjectively to a coarse component of
Z^{c}
.
Then the following square

\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\tau}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{f}}
\textstyle{T(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\tau}_{X}^{G}}
\textstyle{T(X)}

(10.3)

commutes.

Proof.

Let
[A]
be in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})}((M,\mu))
be represented by
A
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)}((M,\mu))
with
propagation bounded by
V
. It gives rise to a matrix
A_{y^{\prime},y}
.
Then the transfer
\mathrm{tr}_{f}([A])
is represented by
B
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(X)}((N,\rho,\nu))

with matrix
B_{x^{\prime},x}=A_{f(x^{\prime}),f(x)}
given by (
6.7
). It is
defined on
f^{-1}(Z^{c})
for a sufficiently enlarged member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
depending on
V
.

By construction,

\bar{\tau}_{Y}([A])
is represented by the family

(\tau_{i}(A))_{i\in\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}
, where

\tau_{i}(A)=\sum_{y\in Y_{i}}\tau(A_{y,y})
.
For
j
in
\pi^{\mathrm{crs}}_{0}(X)/G
let
F_{j}
be the fundamental domain for the
G
-action chosen in the construction of
\tau^{G}_{j}
.
Then
\bar{\tau}^{G}_{X}(B)
is represented by the family

(\tau^{G}_{j}(B))_{j\in\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}
and

\tau^{G}_{j}(B)=\sum_{x\in F_{j}}\tau(B_{x,x})
.

Assume now that
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(f)(j)=i
and that
Y_{i}\subseteq Z^{c}
. Then
the map
f:F\cap X_{j}\to Y_{i}
is a bijection. It is injective since
F
is a fundamental domain and surjective by the assumption on
f
.
We conclude that
\tau^{G}_{j}(B)=\tau_{i}(A)

since
\tau(B_{x,x})=\tau(A_{f(x),f(x)})
for all
x
in
F\cap X_{j}
.
This implies the assertion.
∎

We assume that
H:\mathbf{Add}_{k}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is a traced homological functor.
Then we get functions

\tau_{X}^{H,G}:\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}k\ ,\qquad\tau_{Y}^{H}:\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}k\ .

In detail, let
i
be in
\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G
. Our assumptions on
(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
imply that
\tau_{i}^{G}
is an everywhere defined trace on
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X)
.
Then the
i
th component of

\tau^{H,G}_{X}
is given by
\tau^{H,G}_{i}
.
We furthermore get functions

\bar{\tau}_{X}^{H,G}:\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\to T(X)\ ,\qquad\bar{\tau}_{Y}^{H}:\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{A}}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to T(Y)\ .

.

The following square commutes:

(10.4)

.

(10.5)

Proof.

We form the
G
-uniform bornological space
\hat{X}
as in (
3.1
) and the squeezing space
\hat{X}_{h}
, the
G
-bornological coarse space described in
Definition

3.6
.
We consider the cone
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})
with the coarsely excisive pair of invariant subsets

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(\hat{X}):=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}(-\infty,n]\times X\ ,\qquad\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(\hat{X}):=\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}[n,\infty)\times X\ .

We then have a coarse equivalence

\hat{X}_{h}\to\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(\hat{X})\cap\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(\hat{X})\ ,\quad x_{n}\mapsto(n,x_{n})\ ,

where
x_{n}
denotes the point
x
in the
n
th component
X_{n}
of
\hat{X}
.

Let

\mathrm{Yo}:G\mathbf{BC}\to G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}

denote the universal equivariant coarse homology theory
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 4.9]
.
The Mayer-Vietoris boundary of the decomposition
(\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(\hat{X}),\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{+}(\hat{X}))
of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})
gives a morphism

\hat{\partial}:\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})\to\Sigma\mathrm{Yo}\hat{X}_{h}

in
G\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{X}}
.

Note that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{X})
and
\hat{X}_{h}
have a coarsely disjoint decomposition (indexed by
{\rm Nature}
) into copies of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
or
X
, respectively.
Using excision in coarse homology theory for every
n
we have projections
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(p_{n})
and
p_{n}
to the
n
th component, respectively.
The following lemma follows from the naturality of the Mayer-Vietoris boundary maps and the usual identification of the cone-boundary with the Mayer-Vietoris boundary.

.

For every
n
in

the following square commutes:

The two squares in the middle commute by the naturality of the transfer
for morphisms of branched coarse coverings which in this case just increase the big families.
The right middle square commutes since

\textstyle{\hat{X}_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\hat{Y}_{h}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y_{n}\mapsto(n,y)}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{Y})}

gives rise to a morphism of branched coarse coverings
with respect to the families
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(\hat{\mathcal{Z}})
, respectively,
and the naturality of the transfer as a transformation between excisive functors.
The upper and lower hexagon commute since
E
and
E^{G}
are excisive.
∎

The commutativity of the left middle square is best seen by an inspection of the formulas for the two compositions on the level of categories of controlled objects.

This diagram implies the commutativity of (
10.5
) as follows.
If we start with a class
H{\mathcal{X}}_{1}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)
, then its images in the right upper and lower corners are constant families
with respect to the index in
{\rm Nature}
.
Let

in
\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}k
and

in
\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}k
denote these constants. We let
[(t)_{n}]
be the constant family with value
t
.
The commutativity of the diagram says that

[(f^{*}t)_{n}]=[\tilde{f}^{*}((t)_{n})]=\bar{f}^{*}[(t)_{n}]=[(t^{\prime})_{n}]

in
T(\hat{X}_{h})
.
This implies that
t^{\prime}=f^{*}t
.
∎

,

and

that the transfers
