∎

11
The topological
L^{2}
-index theorem

The main result of
this section depends on the existence of a trace-preserving algebraic approximation of topological
K
-theory.
We let
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr}}
denote the category of pairs
({\mathbf{C}},\tau)
of a
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
and a continuous trace
\tau
.
We let furthermore
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
be the full subcategory of objects
({\mathbf{C}},\tau)
where
\tau
is defined everywhere.
Let
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{trc}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
denote the forgetful functor from
C^{*}
-categories to
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear categories with traces.
Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be in
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
.

Definition 11.1
.

A trace-preserving algebraic
{\mathbf{C}}
-approximation of topological
K
-theory
is a homological and traced functor
H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
with a natural transformation

c:H{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

such that:

1.

The triangle

\textstyle{\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathrm{trc}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}^{H}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{top}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathtt{const}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}}

(11.1)

of functors from
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}
commutes.

2.

We have an equivalence

c_{{\mathbf{C}}}:H{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{C}})\ .

(11.2)

A more precise notation for a trace-preserving algebraic
{\mathbf{C}}
-approximation would be
((H,\mathrm{trc}^{H}),c)
but we drop the tracing in order to shorten.

Example 11.2
.

By
Corollary

13.33
the pair
((K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}},c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}})
with tracing (
13.63
) is a trace-preserving
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
-approximation of

K^{\mathrm{top}}
.
∎

We consider an idempotent complete
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
admitting all AV-sums with a trace

\tau
such that
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
, where

{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
denotes the subset of unital objects.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a uniform
G
-covering.
Then in analogy to (
10.2
) and using
Lemma

9.11
for the components we get vector-valued traces

\tau_{Y}:{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\quad\tau^{G}_{X}:{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}{\mathbb{C}}

(11.3)

defined on objects with component-wise bounded support or
G
-component-wise
G
-bounded support, respectively.

If the coarse components of
Y
are bounded, and the coarse
G
-components of
X
are
G
-bounded, then the traces
\tau_{Y}
and
\tau_{X}^{G}
are defined everywhere and we get
induced maps

\tau^{K}_{Y}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}{\mathbb{C}}\ ,\quad\tau^{K,G}_{X}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\to\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}{\mathbb{C}}

(11.4)

(we abbreviate
\tau^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}_{Y}
by
\tau^{K}_{Y}
etc. for better readability).
Recall the universal local homology theory
\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}:\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}
from
[
BE20a
, Sec. 4]
.
By
\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}\langle\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(*)\rangle
we denote the localizing subcategory of
\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}
generated by
\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(*)
.

Theorem 11.3

(Topological
L^{2}
-index theorem)
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a uniform
G
-covering.
We assume:

1.

\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(Y)\in\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}\langle\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(*)\rangle
.

2.

Y
has finite asymptotic dimension, finite uniform topological dimension, and a countably generated uniform structure.

3.

All coarse components of
Y
are bounded and all coarse
G
-components of
X
are
G
-bounded.

4.

There exist a trace-preserving algebraic
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
-approximation
(H,c)
of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
.

Then the diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{X}^{K,G}}
\textstyle{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}{\mathbb{C}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{Y}^{K}}
\textstyle{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}

(11.5)

commutes.

Proof.

The assumptions
11.3
.
3
on the components ensures that the traces
\tau^{K,G}_{X}
and

\tau^{K}_{Y}
in (
11.4
) are defined. The assumption
11.3
.
2
ensures that the cone transfer
\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}
is defined by
Theorem

8.4
and
Definition

5.6
and that
Corollary

10.14
is applicable.
The transformation

c:H{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}
(from the data assumed in
11.3
.
4
)
together with the inclusions

{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-))\ ,\quad{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(-))

of the uncompleted into the completed Roe categories (see (
8.1
))
induce natural transformations

c:H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\ ,\quad c^{G}:H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}

of coarse homology theories.
We extend the diagram (
11.5
) as follows

\textstyle{\pi_{1}H{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{G}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{X}^{H,G}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{X}^{K,G}}
\textstyle{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(X)/G}{\mathbb{C}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{Y}^{K}}
\textstyle{\prod_{\pi_{0}^{\mathrm{crs}}(Y)}{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}H{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{c_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}H{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{Y}^{H}}

(11.6)

The squares involving the cone boundary commute by the naturality of
c
or
c^{G}
.
The triangles on the right commute by
(
11.1
), the fact that the traces on
{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)
or
{\mathbf{V}}^{G,\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)

are the restrictions of the traces on
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(Y)
or
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
, respectively, and the naturality of the transformation in (
10.1
).
The left square commutes by the compatibility of the
definitions of the transfers on the level of categories of controlled objects and the naturality of
c
or
c^{G}
.
Finally, the outer part of the diagram commutes by
Corollary

10.14
.

Note that
H{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
are strong coarse homology theories, see
Theorem

7.2
and
Theorem

8.3
. By
[
BE20a
, Lem. 9.6]
their compositions with the cone functor are local homology theories.
The equivalence (
11.2
) (with
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
in place of
{\mathbf{C}}
) and the fact that

{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(*)\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
implies that the transformation of local homology theories

H{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ctr}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))\to K{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(-))
is an equivalence on the point, and therefore on all objects with
\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(Y)\in\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}\langle\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(*)\rangle
. Hence by our assumption
11.3
.
1
the lower left diagonal map
c_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y)}

is an equivalence as indicated.

The commutativity of the cells shown so far now imply the commutativity of the cell (
11.5
).
∎

Example 11.4
.

The Assumption
11.3
.
1
is the most general finiteness assumption on
Y
under which we can show that the comparison map
c
is an equivalence. It is
e.g. satisfied if
Y
is a compact manifold or homotopy equivalent to a finite simplicial complex.
If
Y
is a compact Riemannian manifold or finite simplicial complex and
f:X\to Y
is a
G
-covering
with induced metric structures, then Assumption
11.3
.
2

is obvious and Assumption

11.3
.
3
is satisfied by

Corollary

4.8
.

∎

Example 11.5
.

Assume that
Y
is a compact Riemannian manifold with a Dirac type operator
\not{D}_{Y}
of degree
0
with symbol

\sigma(\not{D}_{Y})
in
\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(Y))
.
Assume further that
X\to Y
is a Riemannian
G
-covering and that
\not{D}_{X}
is a
G
-equivariant lift of
\not{D}_{Y}
.
Then one can check that

\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(f)}(\sigma(\not{D}_{Y}))=\sigma(\not{D}_{X})

in
\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
. For the argument one must go into the details of
[
BEb
]
. In particular one must construct the analogue of the transfer along for Roe algebras and show that it is compatible with the identification
[
BEb
, (6.2)]
.

Note that

\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}(\sigma(\not{D}_{X}))=\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D}_{X})

in
K{\mathcal{X}}_{0}^{G}(X)
, and similarly

\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}(\sigma(\not{D}_{Y}))=\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D}_{Y})

in
K{\mathcal{X}}_{0}(Y)
.
We can now recover the classical statement of Atiyah’s
L^{2}
-index theorem

\tau^{K}_{Y}(\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D}_{Y}))=\tau^{K,G}_{X}(\mathrm{ind}{\mathcal{X}}(\not{D}_{X}))

as an immediate consequence
of
Theorem

11.3
.

∎

12
Higson’s counterexample to the coarse Baum-Connes conjecture

The goal of this section is to present Higson’s counter example
[
Hig99
]
to the surjectivity of the coarse assembly map within the formalism of this note.
We consider a discrete group
G
with the following properties:

Assumption 12.1
.

1.

G
is finitely generated.

2.

G_{can}
has finite asymptotic dimension.

3.

G
has property
T
.

4.

G
is residually finite.

In
Assumption

12.1
.
2
we more precisely require that the coarse space
G_{can}
, i.e.,
G
with the canonical
G
-coarse structure generated by the entourages
\{(g,gh)\mid g\in G\}
for all
h
in
G
, has finite asymptotic dimension as defined in
Definition

4.1
.
3
.
Property
T
in
Assumption

12.1
.
3
is equivalent to the existence of a projection

q_{Kzdn}
in the maximal group
C^{*}
-algebra
C^{*}_{\max}(G)
with the following property: If
\lambda:G\to U(H)
is any unitary representation of
G

on a Hilbert space, then
\lambda(q_{Kzdn})
is the orthogonal projection onto the subspace of
G
-invariant vectors in
H
. Here
\lambda(q_{Kzdn})
is defined using the universal property of
C^{*}_{\max}(G)
saying that any
unitary representation of
G
on a Hilbert spaces uniquely extends to a homomorphismus of
C^{*}
-algebras from
C^{*}_{\max}(G)
to the bounded operators on the same Hilbert space.
Residual finiteness in
Assumption

12.1
.
4
is witnessed by a decreasing family
(G_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
of normal and finite index subgroups
with
\bigcap_{n\in{\rm Nature}}G_{n}=\{e\}
.

Example 12.2
.

Torsion-free cocompact lattices
G
in
Sp(1,n)
satisfy
Assumption

12.1
.
They exist by
[
BH78
]
.

The coarse space
G_{can}
is coarsely equivalent to the symmetric space (the quarternionic hyperbolic space of dimension
4n
) of
Sp(1,n)/Sp(1)Sp(n)
which has finite asymptotic dimension
\leq 4n
since is boundary sphere has dimension
4n-1

[
BL07
]
.

The group
G
is a fundamental group of the compact manifold
G\backslash Sp(1,n)/Sp(1)Sp(n)
and therefore finitely generated.

It is well-known that the Lie group
Sp(1,n)
has property
T
.
The property
T
for
G
is inhertited as a subgroup of
Sp(1,n)
.

Finally, discrete subgroups of linear groups like
Sp(1,n)
are residually finite.

∎

We consider the branched coarse
G
-covering
f:X\to Y
with respect to the family
{\mathcal{Z}}
constructed from the data
G
and
(G_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
Example

2.15
.

Following
[
BE20a
, Def. 9.4]
the coarse assembly map for
Y
is the map

\mu:\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{V\in{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}}\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}}{{\to}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{V\in{\mathcal{C}}_{Y}}K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)\ .

(12.1)

In
[
Hig99
]
Higson constructs a non-trivial class
p
in
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)
and then shows that
it is not in the image of the coarse assembly map. The goal of the rest of this section is to give a detailed proof of this result.

We start with a description of the class
p
.
Recall that
X\cong G_{can,min}\otimes S_{min,min}
and
Y\cong G\backslash X
.
The underlying set of
X
has an additional right
G
-action induced by the right multiplication of
G
on itself which induces a right
G
-action on the underlying set of
Y
. For
g
in
G
the subset
U_{g}:=\{(x,xg)|x\in X\}
is a coarse entourage of
X
. In fact, the coarse structure of
X
is generated by the collection of these entourages for all
g
in
G
.
It follows that

V_{g}:=\{(y,yg)|y\in Y\}
is a coarse entourage of
Y
.

The Hilbert space
L^{2}(Y)
gives rise to an object
(L^{2}(Y),\mu)
of
{\mathbf{V}}(Y)
in a natural way.
The
G
-action on
Y
induces a unitary representation
\lambda
of
G
on
L^{2}(Y)
.
By construction the operator
\lambda(g)
is controlled by
V_{g}
. We therefore get a homomorphism

G\to U{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}(Y)}((L^{2}(Y),\mu))
. By the universal property of the maximal group
C^{*}
-algebra it extends to a homomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

\lambda:C_{\max}^{*}(G)\to{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{V}}(Y)}((L^{2}(Y),\mu))
.
Then we set

P:=\lambda(q_{Kzdn})
and let
p
be the class of the projection
P
in
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)
.
Here we use
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)\cong\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{top}}({\mathbf{V}}(Y))
and the description of this group as in
Proposition

9.5
.

The coarse components of
Y
are given by the subspaces
Y_{n}:=G\backslash G_{can,min}\otimes G/G_{n}
and indexed by
{\rm Nature}
.
These are finite sets and hence bounded. Therefore the trace (
11.4
)

\tau_{Y}^{K}:\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)\to\prod_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathbb{C}}

is well-defined. We let
P_{n}
be the component of the projection
P
on
Y_{n}
. By the universal property of the Kaszhdan projection it projects onto the
G
-invariant subspace of
L^{2}(Y_{n})
.
We now note that
G
acts transitively on
Y_{n}
. It follows that
L^{2}(Y_{n})^{G}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
consists of the constant functions and we get
\mathrm{tr}_{Y_{n}}(P)=1
for the
n
’th component of the trace
\tau_{Y}(P)
in (
11.3
). This implies that

\tau^{K}_{Y}(p)
is the constant family with value
1
in
\prod_{n\in{\rm Nature}}{\mathbb{C}}
.
In particular
p\not=0
.

Since
G_{can}
has finite asymptotic dimension also
X
has finite asymptotic dimension and

(f:X\to Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
belongs to

G\mathbf{BrCov}^{s\mathrm{fadim}}
. In particular the transfer
\mathrm{tr}_{f}:K{\mathcal{X}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})\to K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
is defined
by
Corollary

8.8
.

Remark 12.3
.

We will see below that
\mathrm{tr}_{f}[p]=0
so that we do not have a transfer equivalence. So
Y
can not have finite asymptotic dimension in view of
Corollary

8.8
.
∎

The transfer of the object
(L^{2}(Y),\mu)
is
(L^{2}(X),\sigma,\nu)
in
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}(X)
, where

\sigma
is the representation induced by the left action on
X
. We let
\kappa
denote the unitary
G
-action on
L^{2}(X)
given by the right action of
G
on
X
. For
g
in
G
the unitary operator
\kappa(g)
is controlled by the entourage
U_{g}
introduced above.

Let
(q_{i})_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
be a sequence of elements in
{\mathbb{C}}[G]
such that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\to\infty}q_{i}=q_{Kzdn}
in
C^{*}_{\max}(G)
.
Then
P=\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\to\infty}\lambda(q_{i})
. Since the representation
\kappa
on
L^{2}(X)
does not have non-trivial invariant vectors we get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\to\infty}\kappa(q_{i})=\kappa(q_{Kzdn})=0
.

Consider
i
in
{\rm Nature}
. Since
q_{i}
is a finite linear combination of elements of
G
the operator
\lambda(q_{i})
is
V
-controlled by some coarse entourage
V_{i}
of
Y
. There exists
n_{i}
in
{\rm Nature}
such that the parallel transport at scale
V_{i}
is defined on
Y_{\leq n_{i}}^{c}
. In view of the explicit description of the transfer in terms of matrices (
6.7
) the transfer of the unitary
\lambda(g)\mu(Y_{\leq n_{i}}^{c})
is
\kappa(g)\nu(X_{\leq n_{i}}^{c})
provided
V_{g}\subseteq V_{i}
. Therefore
the transfer of
\lambda(q_{i})\mu(Y_{\leq n_{i}}^{c})
is represented by
\kappa(q_{i})\nu(X_{\leq n_{i}}^{c})
.
Fix
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
and let
i
be so large that
\|q_{i}-q_{Kzdn}\|\leq\epsilon/2
and
\|\kappa(q_{i})\|<\epsilon/2
. Since the transfer is a morphism of
C^{*}
-categories and therefore contractive
we get
\|[\kappa(q_{i})\nu(X_{\leq n_{i}}^{c})]-\mathrm{tr}_{f}([P])\|\leq\epsilon/2
and hence
\|\mathrm{tr}_{f}([P])\|\leq\epsilon
.
As
\epsilon
was arbitrary we get
\mathrm{tr}_{f}([P])=0
.
The brackets indicate taking equivalence classes in the quotients in
{\mathbf{V}}(Y,{\mathcal{Z}})
or
{\mathbf{V}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
.
We conclude that
\mathrm{tr}_{f}[p]=0
in
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(X,f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))
.

Alternatively we could argue that
p
is a ghost and apply
Corollary

8.12
.

Proposition 12.4
.

The class
p
is not in the image of the assembly map (
12.1
).

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that
p
is in the image of the assembly map (
12.1
).
Then there exists an entourage
V
(which we can assume to be generating) of
Y
and
u
in
\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y))
such that

p^{\prime}:=\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}(u)
in
\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y))
is the image of
p
under
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y))
induced by the map
Y\to P_{V}(Y)
sending the points of
Y
to Dirac measures.

We can assume that
V=f(U)
for some invariant generating entourage
U
of
X
.
By
Corollary

3.14
.
2
there exists an integer
n
such that

P_{U}(X_{\leq n}^{c})\to P_{V}(Y_{\leq n}^{c})

is a uniform
G
-covering.
In order to simplify the notation we can throw away the first few members of the big family and then assume that

P(f):P_{U}(X)\to P_{V}(Y)

is itself a uniform
G
-covering.
Since
U
and
V
are generating entourages the coarse components of
P_{U}(X)
and
P_{V}(Y)
correspond to the coarse components of
X
and
Y
and are indexed by
{\rm Nature}
.

We consider the
n
’th component
P(f_{n}):P_{U}(X_{n})\to P_{V}(Y_{n})
. In the following we argue that this uniform
G
-covering
satisfies the
assumptions for the coarse
L^{2}
-index
Theorem

11.3
.
First of all
P_{V}(Y_{n})
is a finite simplicial complex and therefore
\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(Y_{n})\in\mathbf{Sp}{\mathcal{B}}\langle\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}(*)\rangle

by homotopy invariance and excisiveness of
\mathrm{Yo}{\mathcal{B}}
. This verifies Condition
11.3
.
1
. Since
P_{U}(Y_{n})
is a finite simplicial complex also
Condition

11.3
.
2
is satisfied.
The bornological coarse space
Y_{n}
consists of one bounded component and

X_{n}
consists of a single
G
-bounded
G
-component yielding Condition
11.3
.
3
).
Finally, as we work with the coefficient category
{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{Hilb}_{c}({\mathbb{C}})
with the standard trace
by
Corollary

13.33
there exists a trace-preserving
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}=\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
-approximation of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
as required in Condition
11.3
.
4
.

By
u_{n}
in
\pi_{1}K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y_{n})))
and
p^{\prime}_{n}=\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}u_{n}
in
\pi_{0}K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y_{n}))
we denote the corresponding components of
u
and
p^{\prime}
.
By

Theorem

11.3
we have

\tau^{K,G}_{X_{n}}(\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f_{n}))}(u_{n}))=\tau^{K}_{Y_{n}}(p^{\prime}_{n})=1\ .

(12.2)

We now use the diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y_{n})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f_{n}))}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y_{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[-]}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[-]}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}(Y)),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{V}({\mathcal{Z}})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}(P_{V}(Y),P_{V}({\mathcal{Z}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}_{P(f)}}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(X)),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}}))))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(P_{U}(X),P_{U}(f^{-1}({\mathcal{Z}})))}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(X)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[-]}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(P_{U}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[-]}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{-1}K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P_{U}(X_{n})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}}
\textstyle{K{\mathcal{X}}^{G}(P_{U}(X_{n}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which commutes by the naturality of the transfer. The unnamed vertical maps are all induced by inclusions of components.
The commutativity of the middle part says that

[\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}(u)]=\mathrm{tr}_{P(f)}[p^{\prime}]

where the brackets indicate the projection to the relative
K
-groups.
We conclude that

\bar{\tau}^{K,G}_{X}[\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}(u)]=\bar{\tau}^{K,G}_{X}(\mathrm{tr}_{P(f)}[p^{\prime}])\ .

(12.3)

The outer part of the diagram says that

\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}(u)
has the components

\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f_{n}))}(u_{n})
and therefore
that by (

) the trace

\bar{\tau}^{K,G}_{X}[\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}\mathrm{tr}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(P(f))}(u)]
is the class of the constant family
(1_{n})
in
T(X)\cong\prod_{{\rm Nature}}/\bigoplus_{{\rm Nature}}{\mathbb{C}}
.
Since
\mathrm{tr}_{f}[p]=0
we also have
\mathrm{tr}_{P(f)}[p^{\prime}]=0
. This would imply

\bar{\tau}^{K,G}_{X}(\mathrm{tr}_{P(f)}[p^{\prime}])=0
which is a contradiction.
∎

Remark 12.5
.

The argument above is essentially the same as the one given in
[
Hig99
, Sec. 5]
with the following exceptions. Firstly, we provide an alternative to

[
Hig99
, Lem. 5.4]
and propose the condition of finite asymptotic dimension as a condition for the
existence of the transfer. Secondly, instead of justifying the applicability of Atiyah’s
L^{2}
-index theorem in
[
Hig99
, Prop. 5.6]

we propose to use our topological
L^{2}
-index theorem
Theorem

11.3

which allows us to argue completely within coarse
K
-homology theory.

∎

13
Comparison of algebraic and topological
K
-theory functors

Let

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad K^{\mathrm{top}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

denote the
algebraic
K
-theory functor for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories and
the topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories. We form the universal polynomially homotopy invariant functor (see (
13.41
))

h:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

under
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
called the homotopy
K
-theory for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
denote the forgetful functor from
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear categories to
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories, and

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
the forgetful functor from unital
C^{*}
-categories to
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear categories.
Finally let

{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
be the algebra of trace class operators on the Hilbert space
\ell^{2}
. Then we can twist the composition
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}
with
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
and obtain
the functor

(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

The main goal of this section is to state
Definition

13.23
of the natural transformation

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.1)

We will observe that it induces an equivalence on the
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
of finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, and that it is compatible with the trace homomorphisms.

In the language of
Definition

11.1
we can formulate our result as follows.

Theorem 13.1
.

The pair
((K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}},c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}})
is a trace-preserving
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
-approximation of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
.

An intermediate step towards the transformation (
13.1
) is
a transformation

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(see (
13.31
))
from the algebraic to the topological
K
-theory of unital
C^{*}
-categories, where
{\mathcal{T}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is the obvious forgetful functor.
The existence of such a transformation is surely folklore. But the details of the construction of

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
and
K^{\mathrm{top}}
are quite different, and so the construction of this transformation
is not completely obvious.

13.1
K
-theory for additive and
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories

The source of all algebraic
K
-theory functors considered in the present note is the non-connective
K
-theory

K^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.2)

for additive categories. Original references for this functor are

[
Sch06
]
,
[
Wei89
]
.

Remark 13.2
.

The most direct classical construction of the connective cover
K^{\mathrm{alg}}_{\geq 0}
of this functor
associates to
{\mathbf{A}}
in
\mathbf{Add}
the group completion
K^{\mathrm{alg}}_{\geq 0}({\mathbf{A}})
of the commutative groupoid
{\mathbf{A}}^{\simeq}

considered as a monoid in anima (realized by the nerve of the underlying groupoid of
{\mathbf{A}}
) with monoid structure induced by the sum in
{\mathbf{A}}
.
Here we identify commutative groups in anima with connective spectra.

Alternatively one can equip
{\mathbf{A}}
with a Waldhausen category structure and obtain
K^{\mathrm{alg}}_{\geq 0}({\mathbf{A}})
by applying Waldhausen’s
S_{\bullet}
-construction.

The non-connective versions
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
are then constructed by applying delooping constructions, see e.g.
[
BC20
]

for a discussion of the uniqueness of the resulting functor
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
.

In the present paper we prefer to define
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
using universal constructions.

∎

Following
[
BC20
]

we consider the functor

{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}:\mathbf{Add}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}

which sends an additive catgeory
{\mathbf{A}}
to the stable
\infty
-category obtained by Dwyer-Kan localizing the category
of bounded chain complexes in
{\mathbf{A}}
at the homotopy equivalences.

Remark 13.3
.

In this remark we characterize
{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}
by universal properties.
Let
\ell:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}
denote the Dwyer-Kan localization of
\mathbf{Add}
at the equivalences.
Since
{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}
sends equivalences to equivalences it has a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.73605pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.73605pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Add}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.1632pt\raise-21.15276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ell}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.39075pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.75577pt\raise 6.74501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.74501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.9443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.84018pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 99.9443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 37.73605pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.1686pt\raise-22.95332pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25891pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty,2,1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.11635pt\raise-7.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 113.3644pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We have a fully faithful embedding
\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{add}}_{\infty}
into the category of additive
\infty
-categories. We furthermore have a forgetful (inclusion) functor
\mathcal{R}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{add}}_{\infty}

from stable
\infty
-categories to additive
\infty
-categories.
By
[
BCKW
, Thm. 7.4.9]
the functor
{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty,2,1}
is equivalent to the restriction
of the left-adjoint of
\mathcal{R}
to
\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}
.

∎

Recall that a localizing invariant is a functor
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ex}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to a cocomplete stable
\infty
-category which preserves filtered colimits and sends Verdier sequences to fibre sequences. By
[
BGT13
]
there exists
a universal localizing invariant

{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}\ .

(13.3)

Let
\mathbf{Sp}^{\omega}
denote the stable
\infty
-category of compact objects in
\mathbf{Sp}
.

Definition 13.4
.

We define
the algebraic
K
-theory functor for additive categories as the composition

K^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}}{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}}({\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}(\mathbf{Sp}^{\omega}),-)}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.4)

Remark 13.5
.

The following properties of the functor in (
13.4
) are relevant for the present paper:

1.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
sends equivalences to equivalences.

2.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
sends Karoubi filtrations (see
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 8.2]
) to fibre sequences.

3.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
annihilates flasques (see
[
BEKW20a
, Def. 8.1]
).

4.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
preserves filtered colimits.

5.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences.

6.

K^{\mathrm{alg}}
sends infinite products of additive categories to products.

So
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
is in particular homological in the sense of
Definition

6.2
.
Based on the definition (
13.4
) the first five properties follow from the corresponding properties of the universal
K
-theory functor

\mathrm{UK}:={\mathcal{U}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}\circ{\mathbf{Ch}}^{b}_{\infty}:\mathbf{Add}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathtt{Loc}}}

shown in
[
BC20
, Sec. 2.3]
.
The preservation of products is more involved and uses different models
[
KW20
]
.
∎

As an intermediate category between additive categories and rings we consider the category
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
of categories enriched in abelian groups, called
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories.
A
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear category with a single object is a unital ring. We indicate the
\infty
-categories obtained by inverting equivalences with subscripts
(2,1)
.
We have a forgetful functor

{\mathcal{S}}:\mathbf{Add}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}

(13.5)

(
{\mathcal{S}}
for ”forgets existence of sums”). It preserves equivalences and therefore has a unique factorization
{\mathcal{S}}_{2,1}
over the localization at the equivalences. This factorization then fits into an adjunction

(-)_{\oplus,2,1}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}},2,1}\leftrightarrows\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}:{\mathcal{S}}_{2,1}

(13.6)

whose left-adjoint is
given by the free sum completion
[
BEKW20e
, Cor. 2.60]
. The notation indicates that the free sum completion
exists as a functor
(-)_{\oplus}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Add}
. But only its descent to the localization fits into the adjunction.

Since
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
preserves equivalences it has a unique factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.73605pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.73605pt\raise 21.1632pt\raise-21.15276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ell}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.39075pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.40192pt\raise 6.24501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{K^{\mathrm{alg}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.9443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.9443pt\raise-5.62047pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 106.33316pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Definition 13.6
.

We define the algebraic
K
-theory functor

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories by the diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 14.85759pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.85759pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.57635pt\raise-26.4511pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.07283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.57635pt\raise-31.34723pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 121.17696pt\raise-31.34723pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(13.7)

Remark 13.7
.

This extension has the following properties inherited from
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
since
(-)_{\oplus}
preserves equivalences, Morita equivalences
and filtered colimits:

1.

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
sends equivalences to equivalences.

2.

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences.

3.

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
preserves filtered colimits.

∎

13.2
K
-theory for Rings and
C^{*}
-algebras

In this subsection we recall the
K
-theory functors for rings and
C^{*}
-algebras. Its main result is the construction of a natural comparison map from the algebraic
K
-theory of
C^{*}
-algebras considered as rings to their topological
K
-theory. Thereby both functors are defined in terms of universal constructions, and the transformation is derived from universal properties.

We have a canonical inclusion
\mathbf{Ring}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
viewing unital rings as
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories
with a single object.

Definition 13.8
.

We define the algebraic
K
-theory functor for unital rings by

K^{Ring}_{|\mathbf{Ring}}:\mathbf{Ring}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\xrightarrow{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Let
\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the category of possibly non-unital rings.

Definition 13.9
.

We extend the functor above to a functor

K^{Ring}:\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ ,\quad K^{Ring}(R):={\mathrm{Fib}}(K^{Ring}(R^{+})\to K^{Ring}({\mathbb{Z}}))\ ,

(13.8)

where
R^{+}
is the unitalization of
R
and
R^{+}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
is the canonical split projection.

Remark 13.10
.

Making (
13.8
) and (
13.7
) explicit for a unital ring
A
and using the equivalence

\ell(\mathrm{incl}(A))_{\oplus}\simeq\ell({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{free}}(A))

in
\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}
we get

K^{Ring}(A)\simeq K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{free}}(A))

which is one of the usual definitions.
Since
K^{\mathrm{alg}}:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is Morita invariant
and the inclusion

{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{free}}(A)\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A)
is a Morita equivalence we also get the formula

K^{Ring}(A)\simeq K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A))\ .

(13.9)

∎

Extending
[
Hig90b
]
from ordinary to
\infty
-categories by
[
Bun24b
, Thm. 8.5]
we have a universal symmetric monoidal (for
\otimes_{\max}
) homotopy invariant,
K
-stable, exact and
s
-finitary functor

\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}

(13.10)

called the
E
-theory functor. It is the analogue of the universal localizing invariant in (
13.3
).
Following
[
Bun24b
, Def. 9.3]
and in analogy to
Definition

13.4
we adopt the following:

Definition 13.11
.

We define the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras as the composition

K^{C^{*}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{e}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{E}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}}),-)}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.11)

As explained in
[
Bun24b
, Sec. 9 & 10]
this is compatible with classical definitions.

Remark 13.12
.

The first six of the following properties of
K^{C^{*}}
are immediate from this definition and the corresponding properties of the
E
-theory functor

1.

K^{C^{*}}
takes values in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
.

2.

K^{C^{*}}
is homotopy invariant.

3.

K^{C^{*}}
is
K
-stable.

4.

K^{C^{*}}
is exact.

5.

K^{C^{*}}
is
s
-finitary.

6.

K^{C^{*}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
is lax symmetric monoidal.

7.

K^{C^{*}}
preserves filtered colimits.

Property
1
is automatic if one defines the commutative complex
K
-theory ring spectrum by
KU:={\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}}),\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}}))
. It encodes Bott periodicity.
The preservation of filtered colimits is equivalent to the statement that
\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object. The verification is more complicated and uses the comparison with the classical picture which expresses e.g.
K_{0}(A)
in terms of projections in matrix algebras over the unitalization of
A
.

∎

We have a forgetful functor

{\mathcal{T}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}

(13.12)

(
{\mathcal{T}}
for ”forgets the topology and
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear structure”)
from possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-algebras to possibly non-unital rings
which we use in order to restrict functors
defined on
\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
C^{*}
-algebras.
The main goal of this subsection is to construct a natural transformation

c^{C^{*}}:K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}\to K^{C^{*}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.13)

relating the algebraic
K
-theory of
C^{*}
-algebras considered as rings with their topological
K
-theory.

Remark 13.13
.

The existence of such a transformation based on the construction of
K^{Ring}
in terms of Quillen’s
+
-construction is well-known
[
Wei89
]
,
[
Ros97
]
. Let
A
be a unital
C^{*}
-algebra. Then we have a map
BGL(A)^{\delta}\to BGL(A)
, where one considers
GL(A)=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}GL_{n}(A)
as a topological group and
GL(A)^{\delta}
is the underlying discrete group.
One then observes a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.08371pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.08371pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{BGL(A)^{\delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.65393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.57063pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 73.36882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 128.65393pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{BGL(A)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.08371pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{BGL(A)^{\delta,+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.65465pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 147.67027pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

This can be used to determine a map

K^{Ring}_{\geq 1}({\mathcal{T}}(A))\to K^{C^{*}}_{\geq 1}(A)

of
1
-connected covers. One then extends this to the non-connective versions of the
K
-theory spectra and non-unital
C^{*}
-algebras.

In the present paper we will provide a different construction of the transformation (
13.13
)
which is adapted to our definition of
K
-theory functors via universal properties.

∎

We let
K
denote the
C^{*}
-algebra of compact operators on
\ell^{2}
.

Lemma 13.14
.

The functor

K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

has the following properties:

1.

homotopy invariant

2.

K
-stable

3.

exact

4.

s-finitary

Proof.

By construction the functor
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)
is
K
-stable.
Since the maximal tensor product of
C^{*}
-algebras preserves exact sequences and
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}
is known to be exact (every
C^{*}
-algebra is
H
-unital by a result of Wodzicki), the functor
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)
is exact, too. It is in particular split exact and therefore homotopy invariant by

[
Hig90a
, Thm. 3.2.2]
.

Recall that a functor
F:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is called
s
-finitary if for all
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq A}F(A^{\prime})\simeq F(A)\ ,

where the colimit runs over the
\aleph_{1}
-filtered poset of separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A
.
The functor
{\mathcal{T}}\circ(K\otimes_{\max}-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}
preserves
\aleph_{1}
-filtered colimits.
Since
K^{Ring}
preserves all filtered colimits we finally conclude that
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)
is
s
-finitary.
∎

Since
K^{C^{*}}
is defined as a functor corepresented by
\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})
in
E
-theory it is easy to construct natural transformations out of
K^{C^{*}}
using universal properties.
It follows from
Lemma

13.14
and the universal property of the
E
-theory functor in (
13.10
) that
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
has a factorization

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.60829pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.60829pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.05133pt\raise 7.09224pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.34224pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.41386pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.91743pt\raise-20.20137pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.60829pt\raise-28.97533pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 46.60829pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.01108pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 113.41386pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 143.41386pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.60829pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 76.60829pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.6311pt\raise-21.12498pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{!}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.41386pt\raise-4.2268pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 113.41386pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 149.80272pt\raise-31.38887pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(13.14)

The choice of a one-dimensional projection
p
in
K
determines a natural transformation

\mathrm{id}\to K\otimes_{\max}-:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

(13.15)

called the left upper corner embedding.
We have a map

S\xrightarrow{unit}K^{Ring}({\mathbb{Z}})\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{C}}}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}({\mathbb{C}})\xrightarrow{\eqref{wfgerfwerfw}}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}})\ .

(13.16)

Together with the dotted arrow in (
13.14
) it determines a transformation

K^{C^{*}}\to K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.17)

In detail it is given by

\displaystyle K^{C^{*}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}}),\mathrm{e}(-))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathbf{Sp}}(K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}{\mathbb{C}}),K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{werferwferwfwf}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)\ .

The statement of following theorem is known as the Karoubi conjecture and was shown by Suslin and Wodzicki
[
SW90
]
.

Theorem 13.15
.

The transformation (
13.17
)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

After all these preparations it is not complicated to finish the argument for the Karoubi coinjecture.
Using the exactness of the functors in the domain and target it suffices to verify that (
13.17
) induces an isomorphism

\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}A)

for all
C^{*}
-algebras
A
. To see this we can replace
A
by
K\otimes_{\max}A
in the domain using
K
-stability of
K^{C^{*}}
and
then argue that for
C^{*}
-algebras topological and algebraic
K
-theory in degree zero coincide.
∎

Definition 13.16
.

We define the comparison map from algebraic to topological
K
-theory of
C^{*}
-algebras as the
composition

c^{C^{*}}:K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{wfgerfwerfw}}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes_{\max}-)\xleftarrow{\eqref{rfwrefwfwf},\simeq}K^{C^{*}}\ .

(13.18)

13.3
K
-theory of
C^{*}
-categories

In this subsection we recall the construction of the
K
-theory functor

K^{\mathrm{top}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

for possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-categories.
The usual construction derives
K^{\mathrm{top}}
from the
topological
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras
(
13.11
).
Following
[
Joa03
]
we use the adjunction

A^{f}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

(13.19)

Definition 13.17
.

We define the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories as the composition

K^{\mathrm{top}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A^{f}}}{{\to}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle K^{C^{*}}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.20)

We refer to
[
BEa
]
,
[
BEL
]
for a detailed discussion of properties of this functor.

Remark 13.18
.

For the present paper the following are relevant:

1.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
extends
K^{C^{*}}
in the following sense: For a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we have

K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{incl}(A))\simeq K^{C^{*}}(A)\ .

(13.21)

2.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
sends unitary equivalences to equivalences.

3.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
sends exact sequences to fibre sequences.

4.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
annihilates flasques.

5.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
preserves filtered colimits.

6.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
ist stable: It sends the stabilization morphisms

{\mathbf{C}}\to K\otimes_{\max}{\mathbf{C}}
to equivalences.

7.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
is homotopy invariant: It sends the morphisms

{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}[0,1]:=C([0,1])\otimes_{\max}{\mathbf{C}}
to equivalences.

8.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
sends Morita and weak Morita equivalences to equivalences.

9.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
sends infinite products of additive
C^{*}
-categories to products.

10.

K^{\mathrm{top}}
has a lax symmetric monoial refinement.

∎

The only properties in the list in
Remark

13.18
which can be seen directly from the definition (
13.20
) are that
K^{\mathrm{top}}
preserves filtered colimits and admits a lax symmetric monoidal refinement.
In order to see the other properties one uses a different construction based on the functor

A:cC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

(13.22)

which sends a
C^{*}
-category to the completion
A({\mathbf{C}})
of the pre-
C^{*}
-algebra with underlying vector space

A^{0}({\mathbf{C}}):=\bigoplus_{C,D\in{\mathbf{C}}}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,D)\ ,

(13.23)

and with matrix multiplication and involution
induced by the composition and the involution of
{\mathbf{C}}
. Here

cC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denotes the wide subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of functors which are injective on objects.

The unit
{\mathbf{C}}\to A^{f}({\mathbf{C}})
is initial for functors from
{\mathbf{C}}
to
C^{*}
-algebras.
The canonical functor
{\mathbf{C}}\to A({\mathbf{C}})
therefore induces a natual map
\alpha_{{\mathbf{C}}}:A^{f}({\mathbf{C}})\to A({\mathbf{C}})
.
It has been shown in
[
Joa03
]
(in the unital case) and
[
BEL
]
in general that

K^{C^{*}}(\alpha_{{\mathbf{C}}}):K^{C^{*}}(A^{f}({\mathbf{C}}))\to K^{C^{*}}(A({\mathbf{C}}))

is an equivalence for all
{\mathbf{C}}
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
The restriction of

K^{\mathrm{top}}
to
cC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is thus given by the formula

K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|cC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}\simeq K^{C^{*}}A:cC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.24)

Using this formula one can check the remaining properties
of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
listed in
Remark

13.18
, see
[
BEa
]
.

The following constructions are inspired by
[
LNS17
]
.
As shown in
[
Del12
]
(see also
[
Bun19
]
) the category
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
of unital
C^{*}
-categories
has a simplicial combinatorial model category structure whose weak equivalences are unitary equivalences, and whose
category of cofibrations is the subcategory
c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
of functors which are injective on objects. In particular all objects are cofibrant.
Recall that the subscript
(-)_{2,1}
denotes the Dwyer-Kan localization at unitary equivalences.
The notation indicates that
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
is represented by the
(2,1)
-category of unital
C^{*}
-categories, functors and unitary natural isomorphisms.
By
[
Lur
, Thm. 1.3.4.20]
the inclusion induces an equivalence

c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}
.
Since
K^{C^{*}}A_{|c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
and
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
send unitary equivalences to equivalences they factorize over the localization.
We get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(K^{C^{*}}A_{|c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{2,1}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}
\textstyle{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}_{2,1}}
\textstyle{{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\scriptstyle{K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}}

(13.25)

expressing
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
in terms of
K^{C^{*}}A_{|c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
.

13.4
Algebraic
K
-theory of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories via
K
-theory of rings

In this section we derive a formula the algebraic
K
-theory of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories which is similar to (
13.25
). It will be used to construct the comparison map from the algebraic
K
-theory of
C^{*}
-categories considered as
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories to their topological
K
-theory.

In analogy with (
13.19
) the inclusion of unital rings into
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories has a left-adjoint:

A^{f}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\leftrightarrows\mathbf{Ring}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

(13.26)

Remark 13.19
.

In view of (
13.20
) the first try could be to use the left adjoint in (
13.26
) and

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 43.20479pt\raise-31.40277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

It is not clear that the resulting
functor
\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}
indicated by the dotted arrow is equivalent to
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
.

As observed by A. Engel (private communication) this reconstruction would work if one replaces

-theory by homotopy

-theory.

∎

As shown e.g. in
[
BEKW20e
]
the categories
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
and
\mathbf{Add}
admit combinatorial simplicial model category structures whose weak equivalences are the
equivalences of categories, and whose cofibrations are functors which are injective on objects.
We let
c\mathbf{Add}
and
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
denote the wide subcategories of cofibrations. Recall that the subscript
(-)_{2,1}
denotes Dwyer-Kan localization at equivalences. As the notation suggests these are again represented by the
(2,1)
-categories of additive or
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories,
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear functors and natural isomorphisms.
By
[
Lur
, Thm. 1.3.4.20]
the inclusions induce equivalences

c\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Add}_{2,1}\ ,\qquad c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}},2,1}\ .

In analogy to (
13.22
) we have a functor

A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}:c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}

(13.27)

which sends a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear category
{\mathbf{C}}
to the matrix ring with underlying group

A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{C}})=\bigoplus_{C,D\in{\mathbf{C}}}{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(C,D)\ .

(13.28)

We consider the composition

K^{Ring}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(-)):c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\xrightarrow{A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{K^{Ring}}\mathbf{Sp}\ .

Lemma 13.20
.

If
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{B}}
is a weak equivalence in
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, then

K^{Ring}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))\to K^{Ring}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{B}}))
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We use that
K^{Ring}
is matrix stable and preserves filtered colimits.
The category
{\mathbf{A}}
is a full subcategory of
{\mathbf{B}}
and every object in
{\mathbf{B}}\setminus{\mathbf{A}}
is isomorphic to an object of
{\mathbf{A}}
.
Let us first assume that
{\mathbf{A}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}
have finitely many objects.
Then we can construct a square

\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{B}}_{\infty}}

where

{\mathbf{A}}_{\infty}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\infty}
are obtained by repeating
every object of
{\mathbf{A}}
or
{\mathbf{B}}
countably many times.
The vertical maps are equivalences in
c\mathbf{Add}

and
the lower horizontal map is an isomorphism.
We now apply the functor
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
and get, using isomorphisms of the form

A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}_{\infty})\cong M_{\infty}\otimes A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})
,

\textstyle{A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{corner}
\textstyle{A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{B}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{corner}
\textstyle{M_{\infty}\otimes A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{M_{\infty}\otimes A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{B}})}

The vertical maps are corner conclusions.
By matrix stability of
K^{Ring}

we conclude the isomorphisms in the commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 50.0273pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.39537pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 88.65923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-16.45613pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.65923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 74.0273pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.0273pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{Ring}(M_{\infty}\otimes A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{B}}))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Thus the assertion is true for categories with finitely many objects.

For arbitrary categories we use that

K^{Ring}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(-))
preserves filtered colimits and that every
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear category is the filtered colimit of its subcategories with finitely many objects.
∎

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(13.29)

The upper vertical equivalences are explained above, and the maps
denoted by
\ell
are the localizations.

The following lemma shows that the construction above is really a reconstruction of the algebraic
K
-theory for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories from
K^{Ring}
.

Lemma 13.21
.

We have an equivalence

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\simeq K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},\prime}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be in
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
Since
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})
is non-unital (except if
{\mathbf{A}}
has finitely many objects)
we must use (
13.8
). Unfolding definitions thus have

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},\prime}({\mathbf{A}})\simeq{\mathrm{Fib}}(K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})^{+}))\to K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}({\mathbb{Z}})))\ .

We have a functor

{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})^{+})

which sends the object
A
in
{\mathbf{A}}
to the submodule of
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})
given by
the column with index
A
. This module is generated by the matrix
1_{A}\in A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})
.
By the Yoneda Lemma this functor is fully faithful.
The composition
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})^{+})\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}({\mathbb{Z}})
vanishes.
We therefore get a natural map

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}({\mathbf{A}})\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},\prime}({\mathbf{A}})
.

We claim that the natural transformation

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}(-)\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},\prime}(-):{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

just defined is an equivalence. We use that both
functors preserve filtered colimits.
Since every
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear category is the colimit of its full subcategories with finitely many objects
it suffices to show the assertion under the hypothesis that
{\mathbf{A}}
has finitely many objects.
Then
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})
is unital and
K^{\mathrm{alg},\prime}({\mathbf{A}})\simeq K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))
.

Since
{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))
is additive the functor
{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))
described above extends to
{\mathbf{A}}_{\oplus}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))
which is still fully faithful.
Any summand of
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})^{n}
corresponds to a projection on some object of
{\mathbf{A}}_{\oplus}
.
It follows that

{\mathbf{A}}_{\oplus}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}}))

is a Morita equivalence.
Since
K^{\mathrm{alg}}
sends Morita equivalences to equivalences, the induced map

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}({\mathbf{A}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{oophkerpthetreg}}}{{\simeq}}K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{A}}_{\oplus})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{alg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}^{\mathrm{fg},\proj}(A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathbf{A}})))\simeq K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},\prime}({\mathbf{A}})

is an equivalence as desired.
∎

13.5
The comparison map

Analogously to (
13.12
) we have a ”forget the topology, involution and
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear structure” functor

{\mathcal{T}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}

(13.30)

which we use to evaluate functors defined on
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories
on
C^{*}
-categories.
The main goal of this subsection
is the construction of the comparison map

c:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.31)

from the algebraic
K
-theory of a
C^{*}
-category to its topological
K
-theory.

The completion maps (the inclusions
A^{0}({\mathbf{C}})\to A({\mathbf{C}})
of the pre-
C^{*}
-algebras from (
13.23
) into their completions
A({\mathbf{C}})
for all
C^{*}
-categories
{\mathbf{C}}
) give a natural transformation

A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}A:c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,

(13.32)

where
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
is as in (
13.27
) and

A
is the functor from (
13.22
).
We get the transformation

K^{Ring}A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{T}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{gwergerfwref}}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{T}}A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{t432r2f34r234}}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}}A

(13.33)

of functors from

c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
.
The functor
{\mathcal{T}}
in (
13.30
) sends unitary equivalences to equivalences and therefore
has a factorization
{\mathcal{T}}_{2,1}
over the localizations.
We have the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 150.48846pt\raise-91.33966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Sp}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-119.9076pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 70.21707pt\raise-119.9076pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(13.34)

The right square commues by (
13.29
) and
Lemma

13.21
.
The upper triangle is filled by the factorization of (
13.33
) over the localizations (a natural transformation, not necesarily an equivalence). Finally,
the outer region is filled by an equivalence (
13.25
).
We therefore get a transformation

c
in (
13.31
) filling the lower triangle as a composition of cells of this diagram.

13.6
Homotopy
K
-theory for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear and additive categories

In this subsection we recall the construction of the homotopy
K
-theory functor

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.35)

for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories.
We then define the homotopy
K
-theory functor

K^{\mathrm{alg}}H:=K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{S}}:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.36)

for additive categories, where
{\mathcal{S}}
is the forgetful functor from (
13.5
).
The constructions are designed such that

K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H\circ\mathrm{incl}:\mathbf{Ring}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ ,

(13.37)

is the restriction of Weibel’s homotopy
K
-theory functor
K^{Ring}H:\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
(see
[
Wei89
]
) to unital rings.

Our construction follows the approach of
[
LS14
]
.
Any unital ring
R

gives naturally rise to an endofunctor

{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{A}}\mapsto R\otimes{\mathbf{A}}\ .

The unit of
R
induces a canonical inclusion

{\mathbf{A}}\to R\otimes{\mathbf{A}}
. A functor
E:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is called homotopy invariant if
E({\mathbf{A}})\to E({\mathbb{Z}}[t]\otimes{\mathbf{A}})
is an
equivalence for all
{\mathbf{A}}
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
. We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}({\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},{\mathcal{C}})
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},{\mathcal{C}})
of homotopy invariant functors.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is admits sifted colimits, then
we have a left Bousfield localization

(E\mapsto EH):{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},{\mathcal{C}})\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}({\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},{\mathcal{C}}):\mathrm{incl}\ .

(13.38)

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is presentable, then the assertion simply follows from the fact that
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}({\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}},{\mathcal{C}})
is closed under limits.
If
{\mathcal{C}}
just admits sifted colimits, then
we construct, following
[
LS14
]
, an explicit model of
EH
by

EH({\mathbf{A}}):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{[n]\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}E(\Delta_{\mathrm{alg}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbf{A}})\ .

(13.39)

Here
([n]\to\Delta_{\mathrm{alg}}^{n}):\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Ring}
denotes the usual functor of polynomial simplices.
The unit of the adjunction provides a natural transformation

E\to EH:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(13.40)

Using the model for
EH
we can see that if
E
has one of the properties listed below, then
EH
has this property too.

1.

It sends equivalences to equivalences.

2.

It sends Morita equivalences to equivalences.

3.

It preserves filtered colimits.

For the restriction to additive categories we get in addition the preservation of the following properties:

1.

It sends Karoubi filtrations to fibre sequences.

2.

It annihilates flasque objects.

All this follows since
R\otimes-
preserves the corresponding class of functors, objects or colimits, and the properties pass to the colimit.

If we apply this construction to the functor
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
, then we
get the functor in (
13.35
) together with a natural transformation

h:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.41)

If we replace in this construction
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
by
\mathbf{Add}
, then we obtain the construction of the homotopy
K
-theory
functor in
[
LS14
]
which turns out to be equivalent to the functor defined in (
13.36
).
Using (
13.36
),
Remark

13.5
and
Remark

13.7
we get:

Corollary 13.22
.

The functor
K^{\mathrm{alg}}H:\mathbf{Add}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is homological in the sense of
Definition

6.2
.

13.7
Factorization of the comparison map over homotopy
K
-theory

The goal of this subsection is to construct a factorization

\textstyle{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
(
13.31
)
\scriptstyle{h{\mathcal{T}}}
(
13.41
)
\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}}
\textstyle{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{cH}

(13.42)

of natural transformations between functors from
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}
.

By definition the transformation
h
from (
13.41
) is initial for transformations from
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}
to polynomially homotopy invariant functors defined on all of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
But it is not clear that
h{\mathcal{T}}
in the diagram (
13.42
) has a universal property.
We will therefore use the explicit model (
13.39
).

The following could be developed for
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
but we will state the constructions for
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
since this case is needed for our purpose.

For any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
we have an endofunctor

A\otimes-:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto A\otimes{\mathbf{C}}

(13.43)

involving the maximal tensor product of
C^{*}
-categories
[
BEL
, Def. 7.2]
, where
we view
A
as a
C^{*}
-category with a single object. The unit of
A
induces a natural transformation

\mathrm{id}\to A\otimes-
of endofunctors of
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
.

A functor
E:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
is called homotopy invariant if the induced map
E({\mathbf{C}})\to E(C([0,1])\otimes{\mathbf{C}})
is an equivalence for all
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
. We let
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}({C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}},{\mathcal{C}})
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}({C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}},{\mathcal{C}})
of homotopy invariant functors. In analogy to (
13.38
) (with essentially the same proof) we get:
If
{\mathcal{C}}
admits sifted colimits, then
there is a left Bousfield localization

(E\mapsto E{\mathcal{H}}):{\mathbf{Fun}}({C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}},{\mathcal{C}})\leftrightarrows{\mathbf{Fun}}^{h}({C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}},{\mathcal{C}}):\mathrm{incl}\ .

A model for
E{\mathcal{H}}
is given in analogy to (
13.39
) by

E{\mathcal{H}}({\mathbf{C}}):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{[n]\in\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}}E(C(\Delta^{n})\otimes{\mathbf{C}})\ .

(13.44)

The inclusions
\Delta_{\mathrm{alg}}^{n}\to C(\Delta^{n})
of integral polynomial functions on the simplex into continuous complex-valued functions on the topological simplex induces a natural transformation

\Delta^{n}_{\mathrm{alg}}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathcal{T}}(C(\Delta^{n})\otimes{\mathbf{C}})

of functors from
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}
to
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, where
{\mathcal{T}}
is as in (
13.30
).
Applying a functor
E:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to this family
and taking the colimit over
\Delta^{op}
we get the natural transformation of functors which send

E:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
to the transformation

t_{E}:EH{\mathcal{T}}\to E{\mathcal{T}}{\mathcal{H}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(13.45)

Since
K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}
is homotopy invariant the unit map

K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}{\mathcal{H}}
is an equivalence.
We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}{\mathcal{H}}}
\textstyle{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h{\mathcal{T}},\eqref{vdfsvewrvfsdvsdfvsd}}
\scriptstyle{c,\eqref{qewdqwdwedqd}}

of natural transformations between functors from

.
