13.8
The twist by trace class operators

The goal of this subsection is finally to construct the natural transformation

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ ,

(13.46)

announced in (
13.1
).
We further show that its evaluations at
{\mathbb{C}}
considered as a
C^{*}
-category or the
C^{*}
-category
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
of finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces are an equivalences.

Recall that a unital
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra
R
defines an endofunctor
R\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}-:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.
Given a possibly non-unital
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra
R
in
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
and a functor
E:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
with a stable target
we define the twist

E_{R}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{A}}\mapsto{\mathrm{Fib}}(E(R^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathbf{A}})\to E({\mathbf{A}}))\ ,

(13.47)

where the map is induced by the canonical projection
R^{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
from the unitalization
R^{+}
in
{\mathbf{Alg}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
to the ground ring
{\mathbb{C}}
.

Given a possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-algebra
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and a functor
E:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
with a stable target, then using (
13.43
)
we similarly define the twist

E_{A}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ ,\quad{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathrm{Fib}}(E(A^{+}\otimes{\mathbf{C}})\to E({\mathbf{C}}))\ .

(13.48)

These constructions are functorial in
R
or
A
in
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
or
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, respectively. Furthermore,
if the original functor
E
is Morita invariant, then the twisted functor

E_{R}
or
E_{A}
, respectively, is Morita invariant, too. Here we use that the functors

R\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}-
or
A\otimes-
for unital
R
or
A
preserve Morita equivalences.

In the following we let
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
or
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
denote the forgetful functors.
The completion map from the algebraic to the maximal tensor product induces a natural transformation
of bi-functors

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-)\otimes^{\mathrm{alg}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-\otimes^{C^{*}}-):{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}\mathbf{Alg}}\times{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ ,

where for clarity we indicated the context of the tensor products by superscripts.
Combined with the constructions from (
13.48
)
and (
13.47
) for any functor
E:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
we get a natural transformation of bi-functors

E_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-)}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-)\to(E{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})_{-}(-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\times{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\ .

(13.49)

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
denote the forgetful functor.
Recall that
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\subseteq K
denotes the ideal of trace class operators on
\ell^{2}
.
We consider
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
as an object of
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.
The inclusion
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)
induces a natural transformation

(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.50)

We appologize for the accumulation of forgetful functors, but they are important
in order to clarify the which tensor product (amongst
\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
,
\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}
or
\otimes_{\max}
) is involved in the definition of the terms.
Specializing (
13.49
) to
E=K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}
and using the equivalence
{\mathcal{T}}\simeq{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
we get a transformation

(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{T}})_{K}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

(13.51)

The diagram (
13.42
) gives a transformation

(cH)_{K}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{T}})_{K}\to(K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{K}

(13.52)

By
K
-stability of
K^{\mathrm{top}}
we have an equivalence

K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}\simeq(K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{{\mathbb{C}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq,\eqref{wfgerfwerfw}}(K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}})_{K}\ .

(13.53)

Definition 13.23
.

We define the comparison map
(
13.46
) as the composition

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{vfsfsvfdv4}^{-1}\circ\eqref{vfsfsvfdv3}\circ\eqref{vfsfsvfdv2}\circ\eqref{vfsfsvfdv1}}K^{\mathrm{top}}_{|{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}\to\mathbf{Sp}\ .

We consider the one-object
C^{*}
-category
\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}})
in
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
, see (
13.19
) for notation.

Proposition 13.24
.

The component

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}_{\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}})}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}})({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}})))\to K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}}))

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We use the expression of the algebraic
K
-theory of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear categories or
C^{*}
-algebras in terms of the

K
-theory of rings or
C^{*}
-algebras using the functors
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
or
A
, respectively.
Using
Lemma

13.21
, (
13.37
), the comparison of (
13.47
) with (
13.8
) and
A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}(\mathrm{incl}(R))\cong R
for any ring
R
in
\mathbf{Ring}

we get an equivalence

(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}})({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}}))\simeq K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1})\ .

Similarly, using
A({\mathbb{C}})\cong{\mathbb{C}}
we get

K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{incl}({\mathbb{C}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{C^{*}}({\mathbb{C}})\ .

Unfolding the definitions, under these identifications
the map

becomes the composition

K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\to}}K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{T}}(K)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rfwrefwfwf}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{C}}}}}{{\simeq}}K^{C^{*}}({\mathbb{C}})\ .

The marked map is an equivalence e.g. by

[
CT08
, Thm. 6.5.3]
.
∎

Using that the domain and target of

are Morita invariant functors and that

is a Morita equivalence we conclude:

.

The evaluation

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}_{\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})}:(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}}))\to K^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}}))

is an equivalence.

13.9
Traces

Recall that
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
denotes the category of pairs
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
of a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear category
{\mathbf{C}}
and
a
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued trace (defined on
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tau)
) and trace-preserving functors.
Furthermore,
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
is the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
of pairs where
\tau
is defined on all objects. The main goal of this subsection is to construct a tracing (
Definition

10.2
)

\mathrm{trc}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}:\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\to{\mathtt{const}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

(13.54)

see (
13.63
), a natural transformation of functors from

{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}
.

We will use cyclic homology theory for
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebras. We have a fibre sequence of functors

HN\stackrel{{\scriptstyle I}}{{\to}}HP\stackrel{{\scriptstyle S}}{{\to}}\Sigma^{2}HC

(13.55)

from
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
to
\mathbf{Sp}

(see
[
Lod98
, Sec. 5.1]
for definitions)
representing negative cyclic homology theory, periodic cyclic homology theory and cyclic homology.

Remark 13.26
.

The periodic cyclic homology is represented by the total complex (involving products) of the bi-complex

CC^{\mathrm{per}}
living in degrees
{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\rm Nature}
.
Then
HN
is represented by the part
CC^{-}
in degrees
(-\infty,1]\times{\rm Nature}
, and

HC
by the part
CC
in degree
[0,\infty)\times{\rm Nature}
.
The map
I
is given by the inclusion
CC^{-}\to CC^{\mathrm{per}}
and
the map
S
is induced by
the chain map
CC^{\mathrm{per}}\to CC

given by the projection
onto its part in degrees
[2,\infty)\times{\rm Nature}

and a twofold left-shift (putting the column in degree
2
into degree
0
).
We will use the further natural transformation

p:HP\to HC

(13.56)

induced by the projection
CC^{\mathrm{per}}\to CC

∎

As the functors in (
13.55
) are defined in terms of chain complexes of complex vector spaces
their natural target is the
stable
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{D}}({\mathbb{C}})
, the derived category of
{\mathbb{C}}
. We implicitly use the functor

{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathbf{D}}({\mathbb{C}})}({\mathbb{C}},-):{\mathbf{D}}({\mathbb{C}})\to\mathbf{Sp}

in order to interpret them as spectrum valued.

We start with rings. Recall that
{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Ring}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is the forgetful functor.
The primary character is the Goodwillie-Jones character

{\mathbf{ch}}N:K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}\to HN:\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

[
McC94
]
,
[
LR06
]
.

The
natural transformation

K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}\to K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is the initial natural transformations from
K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}
to a polynomially homotopy invariant
\mathbf{Sp}
-valued functor on
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.
Using that
HP
is polynomially homotopy invariant
[
Goo86
]

[
Goo85
]
and the universal property of the upper horizontal map just discussed
we get a factorization (denoted by
{\mathbf{ch}}P
)

\textstyle{K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{ch}}N}
\textstyle{K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{ch}}P}
\textstyle{HN\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{HP}

of natural transformation between functors from
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
to

called the Chern character (see e.g. the diagram on
[

).

We now extend the Chern character
{\mathbf{ch}}P

from
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebras to
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear categories.
The functor

from (

) restricts to a functor

so that

(13.57)

where as before
{\mathcal{Z}}
forgets the
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector space structures.

Using the natural isomorphisms

we get
an equivalence

EA_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H\simeq EHA_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\ .

(13.58)

We define the natural transformation

(13.59)

as the composition

\displaystyle K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lpetgertgergert}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle K^{Ring}A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gwerfwerfrefw}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pA_{{\mathbb{C}}}}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle HCA_{{\mathbb{C}}}

We must restrict to the wide subcategory
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
of
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
because of the restricted functoriality of

.

Remark 13.27
.

As it is not clear that

sends equivalences in

to

) in order to extend

∎

If
A
is in
\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
, then we have an isomorphism

\pi_{0}HC(A)\cong\frac{A}{[A,A]}\ .

(13.60)

Remark 13.28
.

In this remark we justify the formula (
13.60
).
By convention the functor
HC
is first defined on unital algebras and then extended to non-unital algebras
A

by

HC(A):={\mathrm{Fib}}(HC(A^{+})\to HC({\mathbb{C}}))\ .

We use the the complex
C^{\lambda}(A)
(the quotient of the zero column in
CC(A)
by the image of the horizontal differential) in order to represent
HC(A)
.
The split surjection
A^{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
induces a split surjection of chain complexes

C^{\lambda}(A^{+})\to C^{\lambda}({\mathbb{C}})
representing the map
HC(A^{+})\to HC({\mathbb{C}})
in
{\mathbf{D}}({\mathcal{C}})
.
It follows that the fibre
HC(A)

is represented by the actual kernel.
In the following diagram we see the degree
\leq 1
-part of the cyclic homology complexes vertically with the zero’s homology added in the lowest line.
The right two vertical complexes are
C^{\lambda}(A^{+})
and
C^{\lambda}({\mathbb{C}})
, while the left vertical complex is the fibre.
All tensor products are over
{\mathbb{C}}
:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(A^{+}\otimes A+A\otimes A^{+})_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(A^{+}\otimes A^{+})_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})_{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Of course, we have

∎

A trace
\hat{\tau}:A\to{\mathbb{C}}
annihilates the commutator
[A,A]
and therefore factorizes over a homomorphism

\hat{\tau}_{(A,\tau)}^{HC}:\pi_{0}HC(A)\to{\mathbb{C}}\ .

We consider
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

-linear category
{\mathbf{A}}
induces a trace

on the

-algebra

in the canonical way such that

, where

see (
13.28
).
We can now define

\tau_{({\mathbf{A}},\tau)}^{HC}:=\hat{\tau}^{HC}_{(A_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{A}}),\hat{\tau}^{HC}_{\hat{\tau}})}:\pi_{0}HCA_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{A}})\to{\mathbb{C}}\ .

In the following we use the obvious adaptation of the
Eq.

10.1
of a tracing to functors only defined on
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
.

Proposition 13.29
.

The map
\mathrm{trc}^{HC}:({\mathbf{A}},\tau)\mapsto(\tau_{({\mathbf{A}},\tau)}^{HC}:\pi_{0}HCA_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{A}})\to{\mathbb{C}})

is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued tracing of the functor
HCA_{{\mathbb{C}}}:c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof.

The claimed naturality statement is observed by
unfolding definitions.
∎

Lemma 13.30
.

We obtain a tracing

\mathrm{trc}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}:\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathbf{ch}}}}{{\to}}\pi_{0}HCA_{{\mathbb{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{trc}^{HC}}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{const}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ .

(13.62)

of
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

It is clear that the formula defines a tracing of
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}

on the wide subcategory
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
of functors which are injective on objects.
Naturality on the bigger category is a property.
Let
({\mathbf{A}},\tau)\to({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}
. Then we can
find a factorization

\phi:({\mathbf{A}},\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime\prime},\tau^{\prime\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\to}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})

where
\alpha
is injective on objects and
\beta
is an equivalence witnessed by an inverse functor
\gamma
which is also injective on objects.
The lower two triangles of the following diagram commute by the already known naturality of
\mathrm{tr}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}
on
c{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
:

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathbf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}_{{\mathbf{A}}}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime\prime}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{k}

Commutativity of the upper triangle shows the desired naturality for
\phi
.
∎

Recall the twisting construction
Example

10.5
.
A trace-preserving homomorphism
f:(R,\kappa)\to(R^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
induces a natural transformation of split-exact (we need this property in order to incorporate non-unital twists) traced functors

such that

\textstyle{\pi_{0}H_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}_{R}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}H_{R^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}_{R^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

commutes.

The algebra
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}
of trace class operators is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra with a trace
\kappa
.
Note that
K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}:{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is split-exact.

Definition 13.31
.

We define the tracing (
13.54
)
as the twist of (

) by

\mathrm{trc}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}:\pi_{0}K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}\to{\mathtt{const}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ ,

(13.63)

Recall that
\mathrm{trc}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}
is natural transformation between functors from
{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}
.

13.10
The trace comparison theorem

We have a forgetful functor

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr}}:{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}

which sends a pair
({\mathbf{C}},\tau)
of a
C^{*}
-category with an everywhere defined trace to the pair

({\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{C}}),\tau)
of
{\mathbf{C}}
considered as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear category with the same trace.

Recall that we have a tracing

\mathrm{trc}^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}:\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{top}}\to{\mathtt{const}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ ,

a natural transformation of functors from
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}
.

The main goal of this subsection is to show the trace comparison theorem:

Theorem 13.32
.

The following triangle of natural transformations of functors from
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
to
{\mathbf{Ab}}
commutes:

\textstyle{\pi_{0}(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}},\eqref{fqweiqwdiew9qdi09qwedq}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}^{K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}H{\mathcal{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}},\eqref{erfewrfwegrgw1}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{top}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{trc}^{K^{\mathrm{top}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

Recall
Definition

11.1
.

Corollary 13.33
.

The pair
(K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}},c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}})
is a
trace-preserving algebraic
\mathbf{Hilb}^{\mathrm{fin}}({\mathbb{C}})
-approximation of topological
K
-theory.

Proof.

By a similar argument as in the proof of
Lemma

13.30
it suffices to verify the naturality on the wide subcategory
c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
of
{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{perf}}
.
Recall from
Section

13.5
that the restriction of the comparison map
c:K^{{\mathbf{Cat}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathcal{T}}\to K^{\mathrm{top}}

to
c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
is equivalent to a natural transformation

K^{Ring}A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{T}}\to K^{C^{*}}A
. In detail it is the composition

\displaystyle K^{Ring}A_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{T}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}K^{Ring}HA_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{sdfvsfdvw}}}{{\simeq}}K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{hrtegtrgeg}}}{{\to}}(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{T}}(K\otimes A(-))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rfwrefwfwf}}}{{\simeq}}K^{C^{*}}(K\otimes A(-))\simeq K^{C^{*}}A\ ,

where the two unnamed maps are induced by the left-upper corner inclusion
{\mathbb{C}}\to K
, and for the last equivalence we use that it induces an equivalence in
C^{*}
-algebra
K
-theory.
Using the canonical isomorphism
A_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes-)\cong{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes A_{{\mathbb{C}}}(-)
the restriction of

c^{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}
from (
13.46
) to
c{C^{\ast}\mathbf{Cat}}
can then be identified with the composition

\displaystyle(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\to}}(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\to}}(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{T}})(K\otimes A-))

\displaystyle\simeq K^{C^{*}}(K\otimes A(-))\simeq K^{C^{*}}A

where
!
is induced by the inclusion
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)
,

!!
is induced by the completion map
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K)\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}A(-)\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(K\otimes A(-))
, and we also used that

\mathrm{id}\otimes p:K\to K\otimes_{\max}K

induces an equivalence in
C^{*}
-algebra
K
-theory.

Write
R:=A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathbf{A}})
. Then the map

(h{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}:(K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\to(K^{Ring}H{\mathcal{Z}})_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}A_{{\mathbb{C}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}

is the map (written without forgetful functors)

K^{Ring}({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes R)\to K^{Ring}H({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes R)\ .

By
[
CT08
, Thm. 6.5.3]
it induces an isomorphism in
\pi_{0}
.
It thus suffices to show for every pair
(A,\tau)
of a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with a trace that the following triangle commutes:

\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(K\otimes A)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{K^{C^{*}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

(13.64)

We extend this triangle as follows:

\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{Ring}{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{{\mathcal{L}}^{1}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(K\otimes A)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\pi_{0}K^{C^{*}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{K^{C^{*}}}}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}K^{\mathrm{Ban}}({\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes_{\pi}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}

where
\otimes_{\pi}
is the projective tensor product of Banach spaces and
K^{\mathrm{Ban}}
is the topological
K
-theory
for Banach algebras. By continuity the trace
\mathrm{tr}\otimes\tau
on
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(A)
extends to
a trace
\tau^{\prime}
on the projective tensor product
{\mathcal{L}}^{1}\otimes_{\pi}A

which induces the vertical morphism with the same name.
The commutativity of (
13.64
) now follows by a diagram chase.

as an intermediate step on which the trace is still defined.
